## Thin slice S4 part A: render split, bounds and PMI caches

- `model::render_geometry` produces the geometry layer; `render_annotations` assembles it with
  sheet annotations. `render_with` and fresh `render` use those same paths. `render_item` emits
  independent dimensions/callouts and PMI frames/datums/labels, including their measured ink,
  text and provenance; appending fragments rebases local text-run indices.
- `Projections` retains HLR by direction/up/cut and the latest placed curves and visible bounds
  by scale/centre/origin. A new source binding clears the caches. View-bounds and projection
  counters measure actual work. Geometry arithmetic, draw order and placement remain unchanged.
- `pmi::Reader` retains the latest report per immutable part and PMI digest. Material, threads
  and frames consume that one report. Repeated bytes, empty PMI and refused PMI do not read
  again; changing material replaces the report after one reading attempt. The standalone
  analysis path already used one shared report; the new reader makes reuse explicit for callers
  keeping an Analysis between edits.
- `tests/render_caches.rs`: 12 tests. Eight omit/tolerance tests (two on each slice part) compare
  kept and fresh SVG, DXF and PDF bytes, sheet bounds, annotations and problems, and assert no
  HLR or bounds recomputation for unchanged views. All eight failed before the cache change
  with actual counters instrumented into the old renderer: it measured every view again.
  The other four tests cover fragment composition/local run indices, geometry cache keys and
  part replacement, single-read material/thread/frame consumers plus changed material, and
  empty/refused PMI replacement. Before the new APIs these four failed to compile because the
  item/geometry split and PMI Reader did not exist. No existing tests were changed.

Native warm rendering, in milliseconds (one timed sample per edit, a kept HLR cache before;
HLR plus placed-geometry/bounds cache after). Planning and output serialization are excluded;
`cargo test --release --test rendering render_caches:: -- --nocapture --test-threads=1` re-runs the timings.
The omit and tolerance edits start from the same spec with its original sheet and scale explicit.
The earlier before harness ran both in sequence with only HLR cached; all view transforms were
unchanged. Single samples include noise (10127's omit differs by 0.04 ms); these are not p90 data.

| Slice part | Omit before | Omit after | Tolerance before | Tolerance after |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Editor mounting plate | 4.871 | 4.625 | 5.343 | 4.762 |
| mfcadpp 10127 | 5.044 | 5.085 | 6.397 | 5.133 |
| GRM-03 | 5.714 | 4.961 | 5.323 | 5.050 |
| NIST CTC-01 (`nist_ctc_01_asme1_rd.stp`) | 11.932 | 9.272 | 10.475 | 9.667 |

For all eight after samples, the edit renders performed **0 hidden-line projections and 0
view-bounds passes**. Each was followed by a full fresh render and another cached render to
verify byte equality and that a repeated render also performs neither pass. Session, deltas,
apply/preview/undo and the native/WASM end-to-end latency gates remain in S4 part B; placement
and Spec `place` remain owned by S3. Cache-policy judgements are in `docs/next-session.md`.

clean; corpus-required `cargo test --release`: **62 test binaries, 577 passed, 0 failed,
0 ignored** (plus 0 doctests); `cargo build --release` clean. Build and test concurrency were
limited to four; one cargo command ran at a time. No process was killed.

## Thin slice S4 part B: Session core (2026-10-11)

Implemented the library Session, transactional Spec ops/previews, undo/redo, typed item/problem
queries, conservative placement options, lossless plan/geometry/annotation deltas, and the
connection-scoped JSON-lines service operations. Projection/bounds and per-item ink caches are
shared with S4 part A. Each edit calls the recovered planner once; internal layout recovery
candidates and the canonical pin plan remain its implementation detail. The current global
planner has no safe partial-plan entry point. Editor integration (S5) and MCP adapters (S7)
are untouched. See `docs/model.md` for the public contract, and `docs/next-session.md` for
unsettled choices made unattended.

Added eight library acceptance tests and one serve test. After the fix round, the four slice
tests check the complete S0-intent Spec sequence (nine edits, eight for GRM-03) against a fresh
recovered plan and render, including the JSON wire roundtrip, SVG/DXF bytes, searchable annotation provenance and lint sheet geometry; undo
and redo return deltas and restore the saved bytes. They assert one measured recovered-planner
entry per edit and native p90 <= 150 ms. Other tests cover preview/refusal/history isolation,
no-plan queries and combined filters, added/removed views, changed sheet furniture with zero
annotation renders, and AP242 frames/datums with pins, omission and added/resolved problems.

Red checks: copied the final tests into a temporary checkout of pre-change `beba65e`, without
changing git state. All eight Session tests fail to compile there because the Session API/module
is absent (`/private/tmp/s4-library-before.log`); the added serve test fails at runtime because
`open` is unknown (`/private/tmp/s4-serve-before.log`). The sheet-furniture regression was also
observed failing before its fix: a material edit was missing from `Delta.changed`
(`/private/tmp/s4-sheet-before.log`). No existing tests were weakened.

Fix-round native latency, release build, sequential parts, 90 measured edits per prismatic part
and 80 for GRM-03 (ten repetitions of the complete Spec edit sequence). Times include apply, sheet
assembly, delta construction and JSON encoding; opening includes recognition and HLR and is not gated. Undo resets the state between
repetitions and is not timed. The maximum payload includes deliberately moving/rescaling views;
annotation-only edits keep geometry. The gate passes on all four parts:

| Part | Open ms | Samples | p50 ms | p90 ms | Max ms | Max delta bytes | Plans/edit |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Mounting plate (bundled fixture) | 147.589 | 90 | 5.202 | 8.462 | 8.964 | 1,178,403 | 1 |
| 10127 | 58.887 | 90 | 4.459 | 7.124 | 8.045 | 923,173 | 1 |
| GRM-03 | 74.188 | 80 | 6.083 | 7.691 | 8.742 | 601,968 | 1 |
| CTC-01 | 694.034 | 90 | 17.627 | 33.476 | 36.883 | 5,202,944 | 1 |

Reproduce with `cargo run --release --example session_latency -- tests/fixtures/parts/mounting_plate.step <10127.step>
<GRM-03.step> <nist_ctc_01_asme1_rd.stp>`. The shared sequence is
`tests/common/session_edits.rs`: S0's JSON files are on draftwright-editor `origin/thin-slice`
and `origin/ts/s0-baseline`, also in scratchpad `wt-ts-int-s0/bench/edits`. The initial main-only
search missed them. The fix round adds the carrier move and side-view omission, skipping the
carrier move only for GRM-03 as in S0. Plate always uses the bundled
`tests/fixtures/parts/mounting_plate.step`. The AP242 fixture
is separately covered by the replay test. WASM/client drag measurements belong to S5.

## Stage C: S7 stateless MCP core (2026-10-11)

Implemented the library's stateless Session-backed API, content-addressed disk cache of
Analysis/HLR/visible bounds, immutable Spec versions and compare-and-swap drawing heads,
12 schema-described tools, `mcp-http` and `mcp-stdio` subcommands, and `serve` subprocess
access. PNG regions/ID overlays and PDF reuse the delivery renderers and embed the Spec.
No new Rust crate: std sockets/locks/atomic files and existing serde/sha2; serde_json's
float_roundtrip feature preserves persisted doubles. `docs/mcp.md` documents the tools,
handles, cache, and draftwright-io `/mcp-stateless/` integration design. Production wiring,
editor changes, the S5 WASM parity participant and real-model evaluation remain deferred.

Eight new acceptance tests in `tests/stateless.rs` cover:

- All twelve tools' inputs, outputs and structured refusals against their published schemas.
- Four-part summary/list/delta byte budgets, lossless paging and oversized-record artifacts.
- Fresh-process warm-cache answers byte-identical to Session; zero warm HLR/bounds misses.
- The complete shared S4B S0 edit sequence through Session, live `serve` and stateless HTTP:
  identical Specs, plans and lossless deltas on every edit (nine edits, eight for GRM-03).
- Unknown keys/IDs, corrected choices, invalid edits, stale parents and concurrent writers.
- HTTP/stdio tool/schema equality, negotiation, origin checks and notification responses.
- PNG crops, overlays, PDF output and exported bound-Spec recovery.
- Content keys across path aliases, cold cache rebuilds and damaged binding refusal.

Before-change evidence: copied the complete eight-test file into an archive of pre-S7 HEAD
(`/tmp/s7-before`); `cargo test --release --test core stateless:: --no-run` failed with E0432 because
`draftwright::mcp` and `draftwright::stateless` did not exist. Thus none of the eight acceptance
tests compiled before this change (`target/s7-before.log`). No existing tests were weakened.
Independent Python jsonschema 4.25.1 validation also passes all twelve tool inputs, outputs
and refusals; all 24 served schemas equal the checked-in files. The scripted evaluation is
an executable acceptance check and exits nonzero when any unchanged S0 task fails.

### Default payload sizes and cache/parity checks

Bytes are UTF-8 JSON, measured by the acceptance tests. Logical list pages request up to 100
records; the byte cap takes precedence. Delta maxima cover the shared S0 op sequence.
The bundled plate is byte-identical to the editor sample (STEP SHA-256
`0336b397faae8d94757b1fb0c4a1eca152428559298c6da66c9134851667781e`).
HTTP maxima include JSON-RPC and both MCP's text and structured copies. Images, explicit
lossless artifact reads and complete Specs are outside this default budget.

| Part | Summary | Max item page | Problem page | Max preview delta | Max default HTTP reply |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Mounting plate | 441 | 1,253 | 667 | 1,431 | 3,412 |
| 10127 | 460 | 1,343 | 317 | 1,388 | 3,362 |
| GRM-03 | 463 | 1,266 | 623 | 1,426 | 3,442 |
| CTC-01 | 456 | 1,337 | 1,319 | 1,416 | 3,418 |

Every default summary/list/delta stays below 4,096 bytes, including the measured transport
replies. Paging recovers the complete item collection; geometry-bearing events remain in the
lossless delta artifact. Fresh processes reconstruct the same Session without retained state;
warm unchanged views perform **0 projections and 0 bounds computations**. Tests compare
serialized Specs, plans, deltas, query answers and renders, not approximate geometry.

### Scripted HTTP evaluation against S0

The exact S0 tasks, four recognition files, baseline and success-condition checker were
recovered read-only from draftwright-editor `origin/thin-slice`. Only check.mjs's import and
fixture root were adapted. Run `node tools/mcp-eval/run-scripted.mjs`; it starts a loopback
HTTP adapter with a temporary cache. The committed result is `tools/mcp-eval/s7-result.json`.
The script knows task IDs/quantities; this is not a real-model evaluation.

| Task | S0 → S7 success | Calls S0 → S7 | S7 request bytes | S7 result bytes | Tokens S0 → S7 |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| plate-callouts-right-pinned | fail → fail | 2 → 2 | 1,096 | 1,907 | 6,412 → 751 |
| m10127-hide-width | pass → pass | 3 → 2 | 616 | 839 | 18,156 → 364 |
| ctc01-fix-problems-near-slot | fail → pass | 7 → 3 | 1,113 | 1,635 | 72,584 → 688 |
| grm03-sheet-scale-keep-intent | pass → pass | 4 → 3 | 1,122 | 3,579 | 12,317 → 1,176 |
| m10127-fit-through-hole | pass → pass | 3 → 2 | 609 | 844 | 18,581 → 364 |
| plate-a4-title-no-iso | pass → pass | 2 → 2 | 916 | 963 | 6,349 → 470 |
| plate-width-in-plan | fail → pass | 2 → 2 | 747 | 1,015 | 6,345 → 441 |

Total: **4/7 → 6/7 succeeded**, **23 → 16 calls**, **140,744 → 4,254 estimated tool tokens**.
All S7 calls are accepted. Fixed context is separately **2,609 → 10,566 estimated tokens**
(S7 instructions 594 bytes; schemas/tool definitions 41,668 bytes). Like S0, per-task counts
exclude judge-only full-plan/Spec/artifact reads, initialization and schema listing. Tokens
are `ceil(bytes/4)` estimates of tool arguments and result text, not model tokenizer counts
or complete duplicated HTTP wire bytes. Full wire sizes are independently gated above.

**The section 6 all-scripted-tasks go/no-go gate remains unsatisfied.**
`plate-callouts-right-pinned` now preserves all three requested right-side pins and all other
dimensions, but its unchanged `no_problems` condition fails: three `annotation_outside_area`
problems extend 25.8, 13.7 and 24.3 mm beyond the reserved right band, plus two leader crossings
with the hole-4 location-Y dimension. The runner correctly exits 1 and records those exact
problems. Do not filter info-level problems or weaken the judge. A planner reservation/routing
fix belongs to the layout follow-up and must retain S3 pin invariance and unpinned quality.
The other six tasks pass, including correcting both CTC-01 slot bands via ordinary relative
placement. Stage D's real-model evaluation was not run.

### Final checks

Final verification used `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=8`, `RUST_TEST_THREADS=8`, one Cargo command at a
time, and `DRAFTWRIGHT_CORPUS_REQUIRED=1` with the prescribed corpus directory:

- `cargo fmt --check`: passed (`target/s7-fmt.log`).
- `cargo clippy --all-targets --release -- -D warnings`: passed (`target/s7-clippy.log`).
- Required full-corpus `cargo test --release`: **604 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored**, across
  **66 result groups**, including doc tests (`target/s7-full-tests.log`). Stage B had 596;
  the eight new S7 tests account for the difference.
- Final strengthened S7-only run: **8 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored** (`target/s7-stateless.log`),
  including complete problem-page recovery and committed delta-page recovery via resources/read,
  with the drawing head unchanged by those reads.
- `cargo build --release`: passed (`target/s7-build.log`). No process was killed by the memory guard.
- Independent published/served schema check: **12 tools, 24 schemas** passed (`target/s7-schemas.log`).
- Required `sed`/`tr`/`xargs` invocation of `python3 -I tools/quality.py`: **30 parts succeeded**.
  `python3 -I tools/check_quality_table.py docs/status-2026-10-10.md target/s7-quality/baseline.json`
  compares the latest complete pre-S7 table: **180 cells identical; no part worse**. The

- Scripted evaluation: **6/7**, runner exit **1**, as explained above (`target/s7-eval.log`).
  The mandatory Rust/schema/quality checks pass; the all-scripted-tasks go/no-go gate does not.
- `git diff --check`: passed. Committed as 22f4c31 on cxc/s7-mcp. No editor or draftwright-io files changed.

## Stage D1: go/no-go instruments and MCP gaps (2026-10-11)

Implemented random-pin quality measurements, a one-call stateless HTTP client and task
checker for real-model evaluation, source-derived cache versioning, and self-contained
MCP descriptions. Repeated schema shapes use local `$defs`; all 24 schemas are
structurally identical to pre-D1 after expanding references. The initial implementation
did not change layout code. No task conditions or existing tests were weakened,
and no other repository was edited.
The driver subsequently recorded the implementation as commit `9b7ee2b`; the
implementation agent did not commit or push.

### Random-pin distribution and verdict

All **270 experiments** completed: seeds 1–3 × N=1,3,8 × the prescribed 30 parts.
Each pins exactly N distinct drawable items through the Spec's `place`, renders the
sheet and compares ink/off-sheet counts with the unpinned release run. Selection is
SHA-256-ranked by seed, basename and stable item ID; annotations retain automatic
placement, while views use a declared relative placement to an unpinned root. The
root/alignment interpretation is an overturnable judgement in `docs/next-session.md`.
The table below is the corrected fix-round rerun. Selected IDs, Specs, SVGs and
complete JSON reports are in `target/d1-fix-pins-<seed>-<N>/`; the original
`target/d1-pins-<seed>-<N>/` reports remain for comparison.
Reproduction and `--require-go` usage are in `tools/mcp-eval/README.md`.

Each cell is **ink/off-sheet**; bold cells fail the per-part bar. Unpinned ink of zero
requires pinned ink of zero. Off-sheet includes `annotation_off_sheet` and
`pinned_view_off_sheet`, with subjects retained so a different off-sheet item cannot
replace an old one unnoticed. The aggregate verdict requires every part to pass.

**Section 6 verdict: NO-GO.** Seven of nine combinations fail; eight of 270
individual experiments fail. Six exceed the ink bar: GRM-03 2→4 (three runs),
cgb245 6→10 (two), and sm-hanger 0→8 (one). Three GRM-03 isometric pins have
a real 13 mm top overrun; one also exceeds the ink bar, so there are eight distinct
failures. Twelve plan-view findings caused solely by margin rounding have been removed.
Aggregate ink remains below 1.5×, but that does not excuse a per-part increase or
a real new off-sheet item. The initial 8/9, 20/270 and 15-off-sheet figures are
superseded by this measured rerun; the tables here are corrected.
There were 895 dimension pins and 185 view pins; the prescribed native corpus
offered no drawable PMI annotation IDs. PMI support is tested, but this distribution
does not establish PMI layout quality. No layout was tuned to improve the numbers.
The plan's consequence remains the constraint-based lane redesign before the larger
editor work.

### MCP evaluation, schema context and cache

`tools/mcp-eval/call.mjs` sends one HTTP request, prints tool text, optionally saves PNG
image content with `--image-out`, and appends exact JSON-RPC body and S0-compatible
content sizes to JSONL (including refusals/transport errors). The online `check` verifies
that the handle names the task's part, loads final/default documents without logging
judge calls, and scores all unchanged `tasks.json` conditions. Offline checking accepts
Spec, Plan and baseline files. The README covers server startup, tool listing, calls,
image review, immutable parents, paging, checking and token accounting.

| Per-session context | Before D1 | After D1 | Difference |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Input schemas, compact UTF-8 JSON | 17,046 B | 16,352 B | −694 B |
| Output schemas, compact UTF-8 JSON | 22,227 B | 21,920 B | −307 B |
| Both schema sets | 39,273 B | 38,272 B | −1,001 B (2.55%) |
| Complete tools/list tools, including descriptions | 41,668 B | 42,303 B | +635 B |
| Initialize instructions | 594 B | 594 B | 0 B |
| Fixed context, estimated tokens | 10,566 | 10,725 | +159 |

Descriptions now explain operations, Spec state/version semantics, escaped paths,
relative placement, detail/paging and render artifacts without requiring source access.
The extra description text outweighs the schema saving in the complete tools context.
The two schema sets are guarded by a 38,500-byte budget test. All numbers count served
compact JSON once, rather than pretty-printed files or duplicated JSON-RPC content.

| Scripted evaluation | S0 | Pre-D1 rerun | D1 |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Successful tasks | 4/7 | 6/7 | 6/7 |
| Tool calls | 23 | 16 | 16 |
| Estimated tool tokens | 140,744 | 4,244 | 4,244 |

The committed S7 report had 4,254 estimated tokens; the actual pre-D1 rerun here gives
4,244, as does D1. Estimates use `ceil(bytes/4)` for argument/result-text bytes; judge
calls and fixed context are separate. No real-model evaluation is claimed by D1.

`plate-callouts-right-pinned` still fails only its no-problems condition: three right
callouts overflow the plan's reserved area by 25.793, 13.719 and 24.319 mm, and two
leaders intersect hole-4's Y location dimension. The unchanged pins and all other task
conditions survive. Exact geometry, causes and why widening the reported area alone
would not be a valid fix are in `docs/next-session.md`. The scripted runner and the
new checker both correctly exit 1. Optional `--documents-dir` preserves judge documents;
final evidence is in `target/d1-eval-documents/`. The permitted diagnosis route was used,
so the all-scripted-tasks gate remains unmet; no lane redesign was attempted here.

`build.rs` hashes the engine's own source, lockfile, manifest, build scripts, schemas
and fonts, with sorted relative names and length-delimited contents. Cargo watches
files and directories. `ENGINE_VERSION` is that build-time SHA-256, and registration
and lookup use it directly. A disposable-checkout source-mutation probe built twice:
a comment-only edit changed the engine suffix `99a24ec9…`→`e9cc1c6e…`, kept STEP SHA
unchanged, and the new binary refused the old cached reference. The probe uses an
isolated target directory so it cannot replace working build artifacts.

## D1 review fix round (2026-10-11)

allow 1e-9 mm of anchor-arithmetic rounding at each margin. Placement and packing
geometry are unchanged. Real 1e-4 mm overruns still report the pinned view's ID
on every edge. The corpus regression also checks byte-identical plan-view geometry
with and without pins on 1000, 10000 and 1004.

The cache fingerprint now filters recursive inputs to `*.rs` source, MCP `*.json`
schemas and `*.ttf`/`*.otf` fonts, plus the four explicit manifest/lock/build files.
Stray files no longer change the engine version. Directory watches still detect
new/deleted relevant files. The stale attribution above now names the driver's
implementation commit `9b7ee2b`. The fix-round agent did not commit; the driver
recorded this round as `e605d3a`.

A reproduced MCP startup failure exposed a separate harness bug: the runner could
parse a partial stderr URL as `http://127`, before the port and `/mcp` arrived.
The runner and live-client test now share a complete-line parser. Its regression
checks every split point, CRLF and following log lines. It failed on the old
pattern (`http://1` from a prefix), then passed. No retries or weakened assertions.

## E1: layout around pins (2026-10-11)

All required E1 layout checks pass: **270/270 random-pin experiments**, **9/9 seed/N
combinations**, and **7/7 scripted MCP tasks**. Unpinned quality is unchanged in all
180 cells and in complete baseline JSON. No constraint-based lane redesign was made.
This is the E1 layout verdict; no new real-model run or broader thin-slice verdict is claimed.

Diagnoses and fixes:

- GRM-03 and cgb245 started free linear labels at unsolved centres while the pinned
  labels used canonical settled positions. This reintroduced collisions and pushed
  short lengths/rungs outward. Free linear candidates now start at their settled
  canonical geometry, with current text, before bounded obstacle avoidance.
- GRM-03's isometric really overran the top by 13 mm. sm-hanger's requested
  isometric region overlapped the side view and four pinned annotations (eight ink
  findings). Resolve and fit isometric ink inside the requested relative region
  before fixing it; unrelated edits use the same canonical footprint obstacles.
- Plate callout columns used 7 mm pitch despite labels up to 32.793 mm wide.
  Columns now use canonical measured width, shoulder and clearance. Explicit pin
  ink reserves actual carrier bands before free views are packed. Free dimensions
  try label slides, other lanes and the opposite side to clear pinned leaders;
  callouts attach radially to their nearest proven feature site, as automatic leaders do.

The width/region interpretations and Python source evidence are verdicted in
`tests/fixtures/known_pin_layout.json`. Unpinned oracle ledgers and all existing
assertions are retained. The three new regressions in `tests/placement_intent.rs`
each failed on the original source (`target/e1-logs/regressions-before.log`):

- `bare_pins_do_not_restack_settled_linear_labels` (GRM-03 and cgb245).
- `relative_isometric_pins_fit_the_remaining_region` (GRM-03 and sm-hanger).
- `right_pinned_callouts_have_clear_lanes_and_free_dimensions` (the unchanged
  scripted task and disjoint columns for equal middle stations).

## E2: MCP call efficiency (2026-10-11)

**Review correction:** the original 16→15 instrument below changed its policy and
batched away GRM-03's sequential coverage. Its efficiency conclusion is superseded
by the fix-round measurements at the end of this file. Historical numbers here are
retained for traceability.

Read the editor's S0/S7 baseline and S7 JSONL log on `origin/thin-slice`. The 48-call
real-model run spent five calls on refusals (two tolerance/fit mistakes, two GRM-03
syntax mistakes, one title key), five extra slot-problem reads after its first query,
and separate list/get/options discovery reads. Slot fixes needed exact item subjects
and `detail`; H7's feature path was discovered by trial. Image and preview calls also
contributed, but remain useful review operations. The available JSONL records contain
counts/tool names, not arguments; the companion result's notes and final Specs provide
the syntax/fix evidence. No new real-model evaluation was run.

Implemented, without changing placement/layout:

- Corrective refusals name valid choices and an executable op for the unambiguous mistakes
  observed in that run. Invalid requests remain unapplied; stale-head corrections stay intact.
- Shared Session/MCP `Options.settable`: escaped paths, every dimension/view/sheet placement
  field, tolerance forms, title keys and fit classes from the same size/kind-aware validator.
  Consolidated hole marks share class choices across explicit member paths.
- Bounded summary `key_items` points to envelope and representative feature dimensions.
- `list_problems.filter.feature` returns all related item problems in one query; default rows
  carry fixes. Optional `inspect_part.problem_filter` batches registration and that answer,
  with lossless paging inside the complete 4 KB MCP reply budget.
- Tool descriptions explain batching, fit ownership, tolerance strings, bare view keys,
  title-number keys and discovery/filtering. Regenerated standalone schemas retain the
  operation definition at `$defs/op` and the existing 39,500-byte budget.

Seven new tests in `tests/mcp_efficiency.rs` cover live/deterministic summary IDs on all four
parts, valid hole fits and all dimension fields, sheet/view options, five executable refusal
corrections and unchanged head, complete feature fixes, batched inspection and lossless paging,
and nominal fit-table boundaries. All seven fail on clean original HEAD `624e3b7`;
the strengthened tool-description assertions in `tests/engine_version.rs` also fail there.
The original fingerprint test passes in that clean before build, confirming the source was
actually the original. Evidence: `target/e2-logs/before-complete-tests.log`. The first five
were also run red before any implementation (`target/e2-logs/before-tests.log`). No tests or
judge conditions were weakened. The new `check-efficiency.mjs` rejects the before report
against itself (`16 → 16`, exit 1), rather than treating unchanged calls as a pass.

The scripted runner now uses summary IDs, offered fit paths/classes and offered slot fixes.
It batches the GRM-03 user request instead of two commits; the sequential edit/scale contract
continues to be exercised by the existing cross-front-end parity tests. Fit discovery adds
one scripted call because the earlier agent hard-coded its answer. Scripted traffic therefore
measures this conservative discovery policy; it is not a measured real-model improvement.

Payload/schema measurements (final code, `target/e2-logs/targeted-final.log`):

| Part | Summary JSON bytes | Maximum default MCP JSON-RPC reply in parity sequence |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| plate | 831 | 3,412 |
| 10127 | 969 | 3,362 |
| GRM-03 | 858 | 3,442 |
| CTC-01 | 1,157 | 3,418 |

The E2 bridge-feature tests also check complete replies: inspection with a requested problem
page peaks at **3,940 B**, `item_options` at **3,006 B**, `list_problems` at **3,004 B**;
all stay below 4,096 B. A requested problem page takes priority over key-item hints, and
paging preserves every problem in order. Both schemas total **39,484 B**, versus **38,992 B**
before E2, within the unchanged **39,500 B** limit. Independent jsonschema 4.25.1 validation
passes all twelve tools, including new batched/default queries and corrective refusals;
all **24 served/published schemas agree** (`target/e2-logs/schemas.log`).

One new Node test guards the efficiency comparator: unchanged calls, a lost successful task,
and duplicate tasks fail. Against original source its new module is absent, so the test fails

## E2 review fix round: call attribution and retained intent (2026-10-11)

The review's major finding is correct: the first report saved one call by combining
GRM-03's commits while adding a fit-options call, so its net tool-driven saving was
zero. Restored the two awaited GRM-03 applies and retained the original scripted
policy of using H7 as the requested task input. The hole's feature path comes from
its summary ID and the documented fit ownership. `item_options` remains covered by
Rust regression tests, rather than adding a discovery step absent from the baseline.

The current runner uses the slot's actual `problem_page.fix` ops, including paging.
A paired `--separate-problems` ablation uses the same script, binary, task conditions
and fixes but requests the slot problems separately. This isolates the tool's combined
registration/query capability; no commit batching contributes to the measured saving.
The original pre-E2 report remains `target/e2-mcp-before.json` (624e3b7).

Additional guards:

- The comparator rejects a report without two successful GRM-03 applies; it rejects
  the original E2 report even though that report claims fewer calls
  (`target/e2-fix-reject-old-eval.log`). Node tests check commit order and prevent
  advancing to sheet/scale if the earlier commit refuses.
- The plate script requires every one of the task's three callout IDs and fails if
  any index hint is absent. Node tests exercise each missing member. It cannot
  quietly apply a two-callout version of the task.
- Corrective errors match the actual refusal against an isolated Session edit, name
  the zero-based op index, and validate the replacement through Session preview.
  A two-mistake batch cannot receive a correction for a different refusal. Multiple
  errors can still require multiple retries. Another Rust regression covers this
  plus valid preceding ops and an uncorrectable preceding refusal.
- Schema regeneration replaces type-only nullable `anyOf` shapes with equivalent
  type unions. Rust's validator now checks unions, including rejection tests;
  independent jsonschema checks cover nullable positive and negative inputs too.
  All standalone schemas retain `$defs/op`. Compact total **38,294 B**, saving
  **1,190 B** against 902a691 and leaving **1,206 B** under the unchanged 39,500 B bar.

The five correction patterns remain deliberately conservative. ADR R1 invariant 9
accepts valid choices or a reads-as correction; it does not require inventing an op
when intent is ambiguous or a magnitude is missing. Such refusals retain their
choices. This limitation is explicit in `docs/mcp.md` and `docs/next-session.md`.
No drafting behaviour changed; the read-only Python fit table still agrees with the
previous options evidence, so no new known-differences verdict is needed.

All **24/24 schemas** are structurally equivalent to 902a691 after expanding local
references and normalising nullable unions (`target/e2-fix-schema-equivalence.log`).
The three new Node guards fail against an archived 902a691 checkout: the old
comparator accepts collapsed GRM coverage, and the new sequential/complete-input
helpers are absent (`target/e2-fix-red-node.log`). Current Node suite **7/7** and
Python quality-instrument suite **5/5** pass. No condition or budget was weakened.

The new Rust tests also fail against clean, archived **902a691**: **7 pass, 2 fail**,
exactly the batch-refusal/index and nullable-union rejection tests
(`target/e2-fix-red-rust.log`). Package artifacts were cleaned before that build,
and are cleaned again before rebuilding the actual worktree. The final source's
required-corpus release suite passed **629/629**, **69 result groups**, zero
failures/ignored (`target/e2-fix-full-tests.log`).

## Stream E3: repeated dimensions across the corpus (2026-10-11)

Measured untouched `624e3b7` and the E3 engine on all thirty quality parts,
twelve additional corpus parts referenced by Rust tests or captured sheet bindings,
and all twenty-two AP242/PMI STEP fixtures: **64/64** inputs.
`tools/repeated_span_parts.txt` records the forty-two corpus paths;
`tools/survey_repeated_spans.py` checks test-input coverage and unpacks every PMI fixture.
The survey fails on an input error or any repeated span.

The new `quality.py` metric compares placed linear arrow-tip stations along the
dimension axis in model millimetres, independent of lane, label and witness
position. Same view, axis, endpoints and nominal value within **1e-6 mm** constitute
a repeat. Equal values on a proven two-face PMI identity pair through different
stations also count, as `feature_pair` near misses. The latter requires matching
part identity; equal labels alone are insufficient evidence. Groups record
sorted item IDs and count surplus statements (`n-1`). No near misses were found
before or after. Angles, callouts and unplaced items are excluded.

Eight before groups occur on seven parts and are all redundant statements:

- FTC-10 corpus and its AP242 variant, plus STC-10 AP242: a hole-row datum
  location and the face-level rung both state 12.5 mm. One location claims both.
- FTC-06: the four bosses' `4× 38.1` height also states the 38.1 mm face level.
  The survivor claims all five measurements; the rung does not become a fifth boss.
- Connector 109: the 130.94 mm boss diameter equals the overall diameter at the
  same radial stations, despite different axial witness positions. The extent
  claims both; smaller diameters and axial lengths retain their own statements.
- Bracket positions, two spans: default hole X locations and basic PMI differ
  only by geometric noise (10 versus 9.999999999979991, and 98.816351 versus
  98.81635164398 mm). The basic PMI claims the default.
- STC-06 AP242: 304.8 mm depth and the PMI's 12.00 ±0.01 INCH
  (304.79999999999995 mm) are the same nominal extent. The PMI wins.

`share` now consolidates compatible datum distances across planners, boss heights
with face levels, and overall/boss diameters; `share_pmi` accepts value roundoff
within the endpoint identity tolerance. Spec-listed items win, then the smaller
ID; claims and omission fallback survive. Distinct tolerance bands/basic status
remain separate requirements. Chain segments and an overall extent have different
endpoints and remain separate statements. Existing tests still enforce independent
size families and bore/step statements.

New cross-family consolidation retains the original lane reservations. Removing
those reservations in an initial trial moved unrelated FTC-06 ink and increased
its ink count by two; that trial was rejected. The final change leaves placement
implementation untouched and preserves the thirty-part baseline ink count.

Every before group has a `rust-correct` verdict and numerical/Python-reference
evidence in `tests/fixtures/known_repeated_spans.json`; `--before` verifies exact
ledger coverage. Python drawings and `origin/main:src/draftwright/annotations/_height_ladder.py`
show its independent location/level and overall/boss statements. FTC-06's Python
planner includes the rung but withholds it near another rung at 1:5; the port now
conveys it explicitly in the counted boss dimension. The dimension and repeated-label
oracles remove their vanished differences and record the connector's deliberate
one-statement behavior. Three known leader crossings against the removed connector
diameter line also disappear; their ledger entries were removed, with all remaining
crossings still required by the oracle. The placement ledger records the matching
8→5 crossings and 3→2 band overruns, with the remaining subjects and distances.

Six new Rust regressions cover the location/level merge and omission fallback,
authored equal/different bands and survivor selection, counted boss/level coverage,
overall/boss diameter claims and omission fallback, basic PMI roundoff and inch
PMI roundoff with preserved authored tolerance. **Each failed against the original
engine** (logs in `target/e3-logs/{dimensions,diameter,counted,pmi-noise}-red.log`).
Eight new metric/CLI regressions cover witness order/lane/label independence,
model-mm tolerance at two scales, axis/value/view/unplaced exclusions, shifted
face-pair stations, group counts, numerical value noise, part identity and the
zero gate. **All eight failed against the original quality tool**; its five prior
tests passed. The before survey also fails the new zero gate with eight repeats.
Existing slot and step tests now assert the same conveyed quantities and claims
rather than expecting redundant standalone lines; no requirement count or oracle
threshold was lowered.

## Stage F / S1 review fix round (2026-10-11)

The two major review findings are addressed in code: a plan-local footprint cache is
shared across dimension, leader and PMI collision passes, and leader candidate geometry
is measured once. The former two full collision/PMI probe plans are removed. The
annotation floor now compares measured candidate costs and pair contacts. For drawable
semantic PMI, preserve the established leader objective without probing PMI placements;
presentation-only PMI still uses feature-aware routing. This conservative limitation
preserves the existing GD&T contracts; removing it made `gdt_band` fail on STC-09 e4.

Every footprint's initial exit is exempt, including a target inside a larger cavity;
a later entry into the same concave footprint remains reported. Bounding rejection,
convex-shape shortcuts and borrowed IDs reduce candidate-query work. Extension warnings
have no corrective ops: they are measured and reported only. The scripted slot editor
makes an explicit opposite-strip decision from a detailed item read, at one extra tool
call; the seven task conditions and their checker are unchanged. Pinned candidate
stations now check other prescribed pins' labels/lines before moving.

New regression evidence against the actual pre-review HEAD: the containing-loop unit
test fails its initial-exit assertion, and the FTC-08 drawing/report-only test fails
because the six hole/bore callouts are charged for containing `f:slot:1`. This evidence
uses an archive, not a detection ablation (`/tmp/f1-review-red-{unit,drawing}.log`).
The new cache regression verifies reuse and invalidation after view repacking. The
performance comparison is now an executable gate in `tools/check_leader_planning_time.py`.

The review's minor observations about incomplete outer wires, residual FTC-09/CTC-05
routes and the broader area/exit objective are accepted, not dismissed. Reverting the
aware-mode score changes together fails the independent notch regression (14→14
actual boundaries) and worsens ink on six parts; this fix retains that objective.
`along` remains a preferred station that may yield to fewer footprint crossings, with
side/lane preserved. Semantic-PMI protection and these limitations are recorded as
overturnable judgements in `docs/next-session.md`; the Python material-exit reference
and footprint limitations are in `tests/fixtures/known_line_through_feature.json`.

rendered with `--formats svg` and independently planned). Compared against a saved copy
of the pre-review latest table: **30 parts, 240 cells, no part worse**. The after table is:

## Stage F stream S2: hidden-line precedence and section linework (2026-10-11)

Base: `308132ba6ebaa6a465de551ea20d22ee03c2df29`. The before table below
uses the driver's thirty base-commit SVGs and plans in `stage-f/before-quality/`,
with the new instrument run against those unchanged files. Existing cells match
Stage E integration (ink 282, repeated labels 17, repeated spans 0).

`tools/quality.py` computes both new columns from the rendered SVG, assigning
edge elements to their planned view areas. `hidden_on_visible` counts hidden
line/path/ellipse elements with at least 1 mm of their underlying path within
0.15 mm of visible **stroke ink**, including half the `part` layer's stroke width
(0.5 mm, hence a 0.4 mm centreline distance). Curves are flattened to 0.002 mm
chord error; capsule intersections are unioned and integrated rather than counting
samples or dash pixels. Isometric elements are included in the measurement.
`section_hidden` counts hidden edge elements assigned to views carrying a cut.
The no-worse checker reads columns from their header, including old six-column
tables, the repeated-span column, and these two new columns.

The apparent solid-grey hidden lines are consistent with proven overlapping
pieces with opposite traversal (SVG dash phase starts at each piece's origin):
10038 plan has 41 overlapping hidden line pairs longer than 1 mm, 24 opposite;
10063 side has 59, 33 opposite; FTC-06 side has 243, 128 opposite. This evidence
confirms duplicate/overlapping linework as one cause. It did not establish that
the solid-grey sub-defect was fully resolved: touching short HLR fragments also
restart SVG dash phase, as the fix-round review subsequently demonstrated.
Reversed and partial overlaps are covered by unit tests.

Placed geometry now subtracts visible stroke ink plus 0.15 mm, with 0.002 mm
chord allowance, from hidden curves. Exact line, conic, Bézier and rational
Bézier fragments survive; hidden pieces are deduplicated within 0.004 mm and
collinear fragments are merged into one dash run. Sections have no hidden items.
The isometric policy is unchanged. Geometry cache placement keys include the
isometric policy flag, and warm renders reuse the cleaned geometry and bounds.

Hatch candidates are split at solid-face intersections along the hatch on both
sides of the cut plane, then kept only with material on both sides. The previous
single midpoint check missed an FTC-10 void near one end of a hatch span:
`[-32.65, 22.99, -33.65]` classifies Out. The new regression checks interior samples near
the ends as well as midpoints and rejects that before drawing. Duplicate
or stray cut boundaries cannot invert parity, and coplanar uncut faces are not
accepted merely because material is behind them.

The string_post catalogue's “top ø1.6 bore” interpretation is corrected by STEP
evidence: that axial blind bore opens at z=0 and ends at z=4; the upper opening
near z=11.05 is the ø1.7 transverse bore. The base hatch already leaves the axial
bore unhatched. The regression requires its two walls to be visible cut edges,
its interior unhatched, and no hidden section items; it fails before on the last
condition. No invented upper axial hole is added.

Regression evidence: all five corpus tests fail on the original renderer
(`target/s2-regression-before.log`), including the FTC-10 material test independent
of hidden removal. Four subtraction/deduplication unit tests fail with the former
pass-through behavior and pass with cleanup (`target/s2-unit-pass-through-before.log`).
The new quality instrument/parser tests fail against the original Python files;
existing instrument tests remain intact. The stricter distance-to-ink test is
also recorded against the original instrument. Final results and after table follow.


The all-principal-view gate found three CTC-02 residuals after the first cleanup,
caused by nearly edge-on conics, not an unremoved coincident support: their minor
radii are 2.97e-9–4.34e-9 page mm. SVG's six-decimal endpoint rounding makes those
arcs double back across visible ink. Non-isometric conics thinner than 1e-6 page
mm now become one straight support spanning their major-axis extrema **before**
subtraction and caching. This avoids both false return arcs and retracing a thin
ellipse twice; its maximum geometric deviation is 2e-6 page mm. Ordinary arcs
stay exact. A fifth unit test fails with the old conic pass-through and passes
with normalization (`target/s2-stability-before.log`). CTC-02's principal gate
now passes, with 426 unchanged isometric proximity elements.

Model verdict on the visually ambiguous hatching (not a part-specific exception):

- FTC-10's upper obround and lower rectangular islands really contain material
  crossing y=23. At `[35,23±0.01,22]`, `[45,23±0.01,22]`, and
  `[45,23±0.01,-22]` the classifier is In on **both** sides. Surrounding samples
  such as `[25,23±0.01,22]` and `[45,23±0.01,15]` are In on the removed side and
  Out on the kept side: these are untouched coplanar floor regions, correctly
  unhatched. `[55,23±0.01,22]` is Out on both sides (a bore). The catalogue's
  proposed reversal of hatch between the islands and their surroundings would
  therefore draw the section wrongly; the retained material pattern is deliberate.
- FTC-06's four apparently floating circular regions are **external cylindrical
  bosses cut as material**, not four cut bore interiors. Their axes are y and the
  outer cylindrical faces are not reversed: centres x=±76.2, z=-158.75, radius
  15.875, and x=±114.3, z=-107.95, radius 11.43. All four centre points classify In
  at y=50.8±0.01. The small coaxial bores start farther back (for the lower pair,
  y=85.09), beyond this cutting plane. The centre circles visible behind the cut
  do not turn that cut material into a void. Retain its hatch.

Probe evidence is saved in `target/s2-material-verdict.txt`; the independent
section tests check the actual hatch against the solid. These model verdicts
correct the review's explicitly unproven interpretations without inventing holes
or altering the cutting-plane choice.

**Native rendering verdicts (rust-correct).** ISO 128-2 gives visible ink
precedence over hidden ink; each hidden edge needs one continuous dash run.
Python `origin/main:src/draftwright/projection.py::project_view_geometry`
retains separate visible/hidden compounds without this subtraction. The base
SVG evidence is 13,218 hidden elements near visible ink, including proven
reversed overlaps; the fix-round evidence also proves touching HLR fragments
restart dash phase. `tests/linework.rs` and the curve units guard the correction.
Isometrics keep the prior policy.

ISO 128-50 full-section practice omits hidden lines and hatches only material
cut by the plane. Python
`origin/main:src/draftwright/annotations/sections.py::_add_section_view`
projects `keep_behind` through `_add_view`, retaining hidden edges; its
`_section_hatch_edges` hatches Boolean cut faces by even-odd crossings.
The base Rust SVGs contain 15,306 hidden section elements. Its sampled cut
contours also leave an FTC-10 hatch span through a void: the earlier probe at
[-32.65,22.99,-33.65] classifies Out. Rust suppresses section hidden ink and
splits hatch candidates at solid-face intersections, keeping only material
on both sides. The independent corpus tests guard this correction and retain
the model-proven FTC-10 islands, FTC-06 boss cross-sections, and string_post's
bottom blind bore described above. This follows Python's cut-face intent;
no dimension, visible edge, capture fixture or tolerance is rewritten.

## Stage F S2 fix round: continuous hidden dash runs (2026-10-11)

The reviewer was right: the earlier dedup/line-only merge did **not** resolve
all solid-grey principal hidden edges. HLR supplied straight Bézier polylines
as well as lines, including backtracking edge-on supports. Duplicate capsule
trimming separated touching pieces by 0.004 mm on each side, so short paths
restarted dash phase independently. The earlier visual claim is corrected above.

`src/linework.rs` now reduces collinear linear Bézier pieces to their exact
support interval, including backtracking extrema, and joins continuous lines,
conics and spline pieces **before** precedence subtraction and dedup trimming.
A final endpoint pass restores trim gaps with both chord allowances (0.010 mm).
It preserves curved pieces and source lines via explicit short connectors;
matching conics remain on their exact ellipse support. Closed runs cannot join
into double-traced loops. Endpoint indexing and source-order candidate selection
keep the result deterministic. Tolerances and numerical-support choices are
recorded as overturnable in `docs/next-session.md`. Isometric policy is unchanged.

Two new unit tests cover touching short line/Bézier chains, backtracking
polylines, reversed conic/Bézier/rational chains, and duplicate full ellipses.
The new corpus regression checks 10063's side-channel vertical runs, including
straight Bézier paths. Both chain units and the corpus test fail on the earlier
implementation (`target/s2-fix-before-units.log`, `s2-fix-before-corpus.log`).
The independent `tools/check_hidden_runs.py` checks rendered principal-view
line and collinear M/L path elements against the actual SVG dash length; it
rejects the earlier sheets and accepts the corrected thirty. It adds no shared
quality columns or parser changes. The header-driven checker stays untouched
for the S1 merge; retain a single version there, including repeated_span.

| Principal-view SVG check | Earlier S2 | Fix round |
|---|---:|---:|
| Straight hidden path elements | 62,994 | 9,926 |
| Paths shorter than one dash | 60,187 | 6,405 |
| Touching short-path pairs | 42,014 | **0** |

The remaining short paths are not touching collinear chains. Exact curved
chains are covered by the unit tests. The SVG check also exposed backtracking
polylines and gaps beyond exactly twice the trim tolerance; those were fixed,
not excluded from the gate. These counts are path elements/pairs, not the
reviewer's element-endpoint counts, so the totals need not match that survey.

All thirty parts retain **every cell** of the prior after table above:
off_sheet 0, repeated_label 17, repeated_measure 0, views_overlap 0,
view_on_title_block 0, ink 282, repeated_span 0, hidden_on_visible 2,263,
section_hidden 0. `check_quality_table.py` passes **30 parts / 270 cells**.
`check_linework.py` confirms **principal hidden_on_visible 0, section_hidden 0**;
the 2,263 proximity cases are still in the unchanged isometrics.
The unused `tests/fixtures/known_linework.json` is removed; its ISO/material
verdicts now live in the prior S2 entry. Capture comparisons need no exception.

## Stage F integration (2026-10-11)

### Review that chose the streams

A draftsman review of all thirty quality sheets at base `308132b`
(`stage-f/defects.md`) catalogued 18 defect classes, ranked by worst severity
(wrong 3, unclear 2, untidy 1) times parts affected. Only GRM-05 was judged a good
sheet. Classes and parts affected: title block incomplete / no projection symbol (30),
features without size or location (22), leader or extension line through the part or
another feature (21 + plate fixture), hidden line over a coincident visible edge (30),
no centre lines or marks (30), dimension to a hidden edge (14), dimension text away
from its span (16), ASME PMI not presented (10), baseline towers (9), duplicate or
closed-chain dimensions (7), section views drawn wrongly (6), non-standard callout
wording (6), count prefixes / fragmented hole callouts (8), scale too small or sheet
unbalanced (8), cramped clusters needing a detail view (5), isometric with hidden lines
and too large (10), poor principal-view choice (4), inconsistent decimal precision (6).
Before Stage F only ink partly measured classes 7 and 9, and repeated metrics class 10
on one part; no other class had a metric.

Three streams were chosen: S1 class 3 (leaders/extensions through features), S2
classes 4 and 11 (line precedence and section line-work), S3 class 7 (text on span).
Title block, missing feature dimensions, centre lines, dimensions to hidden edges,
PMI and baseline towers were deferred with reasons in the catalogue.

### What merged

Merged into `main`, in order, each with `--no-ff`:

| Stream | Branch | Implemented by | Review rounds |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S1 leaders and extension lines through features | `cxf/f1-s1-lines-through-feature` (f9d7994, 0171898) | Codex (82 min + 31 min fix) | 2: round 1 two major (planning time, tip/origin exits counted as crossings) and five minor; fixed; round 2 passed with minor notes |
| S2 hidden-line precedence and section line-work | `cxf/f2-s2-linework-precedence` (2c8e2a5, 3f0b10a) | Codex (79 min + 57 min fix) | 2: round 1 one major (long hidden edges split into short pieces drawing solid grey) and two minor; fixed; round 2 passed with minor notes |

S3 (`cxf/f3-s3-dim-text-on-span`) was **not merged**: its review did not reach an
acceptable state.

Conflicts: `docs/next-session.md` and this file (both sections kept, merge order),
and the three quality tools, where each stream added its own column(s). All three new
columns are kept in `tools/quality.py`'s table and `tools/check_quality_table.py`'s
key list. The checker keeps S1's header-driven version (per-column dicts, compares
only columns present in both the table and the baseline); its header detection now
accepts a `part` row containing either `ink` or `off_sheet`, so both streams' checker
tests pass. In `tools/test_quality.py` both streams' test classes are kept; S1's
mocked-subprocess crossing test now writes an empty `part.svg`, because S2's SVG
metric reads the rendered sheet (fixture only; assertion unchanged). Problem codes are
free strings in the MCP schemas and only S1 added kinds, so no schema regeneration was
needed; no known-difference record was changed by both streams.

### Verification on the merged tree

Cargo commands serial, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=8`, `RUST_TEST_THREADS=8`:

- `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --release -- -D warnings`: pass.
- Required-corpus `cargo test --release`: **71 result groups, 664 passed, 0 failed,
  0 ignored**. Python quality tests **20/20**.
- Quality on the thirty parts (`target/fint/quality/`): `check_quality_table.py`
  reports **no part worse** against S2's final table (270 cells), S1's final table
  (240 cells) and the Stage E table (180 cells). The merged table is exactly S1's
  after columns plus S2's after columns (ink **278**, repeated labels **17**,
  off-sheet **0**, repeated spans **0**, line_through_feature **212**,
  hidden_on_visible **2263**, all isometric, section_hidden **0**).
  `check_linework.py`: principal hidden_on_visible 0, section_hidden 0.
  `check_hidden_runs.py`: 94 principal views, **0 touching short-path pairs**.
- Random-pin grid, seeds 1-3 by N 1/3/8 with `--require-go` against the merged
  baseline: **all 9 invocations exit 0, 270/270 GO, 0 off-sheet**. Pinned ink totals
  269/261/261/276/230/234/278/267/245 (base 278). **Verdict: GO.**
- Scripted MCP evaluation (`target/fint/mcp-eval.json`): **7/7 tasks succeed, 16 tool
  calls**, 5,669 estimated tool tokens (bytes/4). The extra call against Stage E's 15 is
  S1's: the CTC-01 slot task now reads the flagged dimension with `get_item` and sets
  the opposite strip itself, because `extension_through_feature` carries no fix.
  Scripted instrument only; no real-model run.

### New metrics, before and after (thirty parts)

Open items carried from the streams (in `docs/next-session.md`): the crossing metric
misses concave-outline crossings (bounding-box approximation) and exempts the tip's
first exit, so it undercounts class 3; FTC-09 and CTC-05 leaders still cross features;
CTC-04 planning is about 2x base; FTC-08 plan's `2x ø5.8`/`2x ø6.3` labels run
together; parallel hidden edges 0.01-0.25 mm apart are not deduplicated; FTC-06
Section A-A shows visible circles inside hatched discs (pre-existing); the
string_post catalogue entry should be corrected rather than read as fixed; and
hidden_on_visible's principal-view zero is enforced only by `check_linework.py`.


## Stage G1: honest completeness and muted lint ledgers (2026-10-11)

The Plan and inspect summary report bounded status and raw completeness counts.
Single-solid unclaimed geometry and unplanned features warn; multiple solids keep
Python's informational severity. Envelope and turned-band ledgers now run in lint,
and patterns owe their location axes and pitch/PCD. `dims::fmt(-0.04)` prints `0.0`.
No dimension planner or rule YAML is changed. The unclaimed-face warning explicitly
says stock faces can be unclaimed: this is evidence for review, not recognition recall.

The 30 default SVGs are byte-identical before and after, including embedded Spec.

## Stage G / G5 — title block (2026-10-11)

Maintainer decisions: draw the projection symbol; leave GEN. TOL. blank without
an authored Spec or usable PMI tolerance and flag it; warn on contradictory Spec
and model materials while keeping the Spec. The native default layout is third
angle (plan above front, right side at the right). Its cone and concentric end
view now occupy the reserved 16 mm band beside SCALE, with a 3 mm large diameter
and black 0.15 mm strokes. Resolved curves and the method live in the Plan;
rendering draws that furniture without resolving drafting intent. Old serialized
plans can omit the new optional field.

The blank-tolerance problem is a sheet warning with a `sheet/title/tolerance` set
fix, visible through the existing editor/MCP problem queries. No engineering
magnitude is safe to infer, so the offered `UNSPECIFIED` is an explicit author
acknowledgement, explained in the message, rather than automatic title content.
A nonblank authored value or unique usable PMI value fills the cell. Material
comparison trims only surrounding whitespace; the warning includes both original
values, the material source and `sheet/title/material`. No automatic material fix
chooses between requirements.

Rules ported: **ARCH0018-1** (projection-symbol portion), **ISSUE-1157-a**
(translated by the new blank-and-warning decision), and
**LINT-code-step_material_disagreement** (at planning, where Spec intent is
available). Python evidence and verdicts are in
`tests/fixtures/known_title_block.json`: draftwright origin/main
`4dfc63f0693bffa0e0e2836d254f53e15cce6f9f`, helper
`577ed47ca68f0d61a39d26ca33ce2c35117f21eb`. Catalogue YAML, dimension planners,
centreline modules and `tools/mcp-eval/tasks.json` are untouched.

Four added Rust regressions were run against the original **b999b13** source
before implementation, and each failed:

| Regression | Original failure | Coverage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `projection_symbol_states_third_angle_and_clears_the_reserved_band` | missing rendered symbol | A4/A3 view convention, reference proportions, scale clearance, band, Plan round-trip |
| `a_blank_general_tolerance_is_flagged_with_an_explicit_acknowledgement_fix` | missing problem | absent/empty/whitespace, exact warning/fix, applied fix, explicit standard |
| `pmi_supplies_the_general_tolerance_but_a_spec_blank_still_needs_attention` | missing blank-cell problem | unique PMI, authored blank precedence, ambiguous PMI |
| `a_spec_material_contradiction_warns_with_both_values_and_keeps_the_spec` | missing contradiction problem | both values, source/path, Spec precedence, blank override, trimmed match, ambiguous assembly |

`tools/check_title_block.py` independently rejects the original 30-sheet output
at the first missing symbol. It checks actual exported strokes, concentric
circles, taper, band containment and the blank-cell warning/fix. Red evidence:
`target/g5-evidence/red-{tests,title,material,svg}.log`. Existing migration, model
and service oracles now assert the additional furniture/warning explicitly;
their original field comparisons and lint assertions are retained.

Not done / overturnable: first-angle planning and enforcement of unconventional
authored principal-view relations remain outside this symbol port. `UNSPECIFIED`
does not establish manufacturing completeness. A blank material override is
conservatively warned (Python exempts it); no material synonym equivalence is
inferred. These decisions and the two transient check failures are recorded in
`docs/next-session.md`.

Files changed by G5 (16): `src/{title_block,model,output}.rs`;
`tests/{title_block,title_block_material,pmi_labels,identity,model,serve}.rs`;
`tests/fixtures/known_title_block.json`;
`tests/fixtures/identity/plate.title_furniture.json`; `tools/check_title_block.py`;
`docs/{model,mcp,next-session,status-2026-10-10}.md`. No commit or push was made.

release tests **668/668 (71 groups, none ignored)** and release build pass.
Sessions **8/8** (including the unchanged 150 ms gate), stateless **8/8**, render

**8/8**. Scripted MCP **7/7**, **16 calls**, **5694 estimated content tokens**;

The first complete-gate attempt exposed an obsolete service empty-problem

## Stage G / G6 — ISO 128 centre furniture (2026-10-11)

Centre crosses, side-on axes, global turning axes and radial/tangential bolt-circle
furniture are planned as each view's `line_work`, independently of dimensional
Spec intent. The renderer emits thin long-dash-dot ink in SVG/PDF/PNG/DXF beneath
visible and hidden edges. Centre furniture and the finite section cutting line
are fixed ink for annotation placement; string_post's callouts keep clear of A-A.
The drawing-dependent centre-mark lint is enabled and reads actually drawn marks.

Rules ported: ARCH0016-4, LINT-code-feature_no_centermark, ISSUE-1613-a
(the circular-seat axis), ISSUE-1166-b and ARCH0014-1 (fixed-ink obstacle hook).
No rule catalogue YAML, title-block code or dimensional-family planner changed.
The only GDT-file edit initializes the new optional view field. The agent did not
commit/push; the driver committed this attempt as `0dbba2f`.

Eight new Rust regressions cover a plate's marks and drawing lint, omitted
turned dimensions and 5 mm global-axis extents, radial/tangential pitch circles,
a section's finite A-A obstacle and leader clearance, the global-tip exemption,
generic circle evidence/overruns (including rejection of a rectangle's provisional
small circle), profiled double-D side-axis depth plus partial/removed section coverage, and oblique opening ellipses with projected side axes. The circular-seat issue
regression also now requires its axis. One new Python instrument regression
requires a rendered structural centre-line contact to increase ink.

Red evidence: the four integration tests failed on unchanged pre-G6 Rust
(`target/g6/red.log`). The initial two units and strengthened seat test failed
with their added behaviour removed (`red-disabled.log`,
`red-issue_regressions-disabled.log`); the rectangle and profiled-bore cases
failed before their respective fixes (`red-small-circle.log`,
`red-profiled-axis.log`, `red-section-depth.log`, `red-oblique.log`,
`red-profiled-position.log`). The Python check failed before INK inclusion and again
before review was fetched (`red-quality-metric.log`, `red-quality-review.log`).
No existing assertion was dropped. Expected geometry changes are recorded with
evidence in `tests/fixtures/known_centrelines.json`: plate migration replays the
independent v2 Spec oracle; FTC-06 removes a stale directional collision; CGB245's
rejected 1:1 attempt reports its four genuinely off-sheet axes as well as its
three existing hard findings.

Overturnable judgments and Python comparisons are in `docs/next-session.md`,
`docs/model.md` and `tests/fixtures/known_centrelines.json`. They include the
maintainer's ~3 mm cross extension versus current Python's ~1 mm, native pitch
circle inference only from an entire group of at least five equal coplanar holes,
round-feature corroboration for smaller recorded circles, local transverse global
axis attachments, and actual text boxes for label/centre-line lint. These are
generic rules; no part-specific placement or feature-selection case was added.

Final validation: fmt check and strict release Clippy passed; corpus-required
release tests **672/672, 69 nonempty groups, zero ignored**; release build
passed. Python instrument **21/21**, Node client **8/8**, render caches **12/12**
and sessions **8/8**, including the existing 150 ms NIST gate. The complete
final suite passed; one earlier stateless HTTP early-rejection connection reset
passed unchanged on recheck (no transport test or assertion was weakened).

Quality: **30 parts / 300 cells, no part worse** against the latest Stage F
table. The final instrument also reads actual structural label/centre-line
contacts, so the stricter ink measurement remains 278. All 23 furniture/section
reviews have **zero missing centre marks and zero label/centre-line contacts**.
Planned benchmark furniture: {'axis': 481, 'cross': 343, 'cutting_plane': 7, 'pitch_circle': 1}. Feature crossings improve only
in CTC-03 (8 to 7) and FTC-09 (14 to 13); all other cells are unchanged.

Visual verification: all thirty default sheets rasterised at width 2400 and
inspected; nine authored-omission variants were also inspected, three parts for
each envelope, band length/OD and pattern location/pitch finding family.
`tools/check_completeness.py` creates these variants from real measurement owners
and checks review findings. Its run against the preserved old binary fails on
GRM-03's missing overall extent. The unchanged default FTC08 sheet lacks pattern2
Y=167.0, and FTC09 lacks pattern2 X=76.2 and pattern3 X=123.2; the new independent
lint finds these dropped statements. Detailed verdicts and raster evidence paths
are in `tests/fixtures/known_completeness.json`.

GRM-03's default chain 0.5 + 2 + 3 + 18 = 23.5 proves its axial extent under the
existing ledger; an additional overall label is not required. Spool132 has six
new numerical pattern-centre location findings, but its apparent concentricity
makes the draftsman verdict **undetermined** pending a certified centreline
carrier. The warning evidence is retained and that limitation is recorded.

Acceptance regressions are `tests/completeness.rs` (nine), `tools/test_quality.py`
(three new completeness tests), and `tools/check_completeness.py` (controlled
physical omissions). All nine Rust regressions failed on an archive of the
original source; all three new Python tests failed on the original tools. The
omission script failed on the preserved old release executable. Red-run logs
are preserved under `target/g1-checks/red-{rust,python,evidence}.log`.

G1 rule predicates implemented: bounded status/face claim/unplanned-feature
reporting supports ADR5-5, ADR5-13, ADR5-17, ISSUE-1392-a, ADR3-7 and ISSUE-1460-a.
Raw completeness columns support the completeness component of
LINT-quality-four_components; the other audited-score components and independent
expected-feature denominator are not claimed. Enabled findings cover
LINT-envelope_coverage-overall_extents, LINT-turned_step_coverage-length_and_od,
LINT-code-axial_length_missing, ISSUE-1560-a and ISSUE-1126-a. Pattern location and
pitch checks cover LINT-hole_coverage-pattern_location,
LINT-hole_coverage-pattern_spacing, ISSUE-1143-a and ISSUE-1143-b. Negative rounded
zero is fixed for ISSUE-1349-b. This describes implemented predicates, not a
claim that every broader catalogue rule is fully ported; no catalogue YAML changed.

## Stage G1 fix round: member-ordinate pattern evidence (2026-10-11)

The reviewer was correct: the original independent pattern lint credited only
pattern-owned marks and falsely warned on twelve requirements already stated by
member-hole ordinates. The original visual ledger omitted those warnings. The
fix credits exact member IDs and truthful printed datum distances. Every member's
ordinates prove location and spacing; one fully located physical member plus
printed pitches also suffices (a grid's anchor route requires a proved lattice).
Fully located rotated grids in principal planes are supported too. Bolt circles
retain their centre/PCD requirements. No planner, rule YAML or task condition changed.

| Default sheet | Pattern warnings before | After | Verdict |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| nist_ctc_02_asme1_rc | 6 | 0 | Members of patterns 9/11 are fully located by x/y ordinates. |
| nist_ctc_05_asme1_rd | 6 | 0 | Holes 11..16 are fully located by y/z ordinates. |
| nist_ftc_08_asme1_rc | 1 | 1 | Pattern 2 Y=167.0 remains unstated. |
| nist_ftc_09_asme1_rd | 2 | 2 | Pattern 2 X=76.2 and pattern 3 X=123.2 remain unstated. |
| Other 26 quality sheets | 0 | 0 | No additional pattern findings. |
| **Total** | **15** | **3** | **Twelve false warnings removed; three true gaps preserved.** |

`tools/check_completeness.py` now inventories all thirty default reviews and fails
unless exactly those three findings remain. Against the preserved reviewed binary
it fails with all fifteen warnings (`target/g1-fix-checks/red-evidence.log` and
`target/g1-fix-red-evidence/pattern-findings.json`). On the final release binary the
inventory and existing three-per-class omission gates pass. Five new Rust
regressions cover member-only grids, one member plus pitches, linear arrays, both
native NIST drawings, and rotated grids. Four fail on the reviewed source; the
rotated-grid test fails before its final one-line extension. Incorrect printed
ordinates, incomplete member sets, incorrect pitch text and split member anchors
remain insufficient evidence. Red logs: `red.log`, `red-rotated-grid.log`.

Fresh release renders of all thirty parts were rasterised at width 2400. By eye,
CTC05's side-view ordinates 230.7/238.4/246.2 and 171.4/206.4/241.3 locate all six
members. CTC02's drawn x positions 265.4/266/315/365/414.6 and y positions 168/492
locate the two sets. The ladders remain crowded; this is reporting correction,
not a legibility improvement. Default FTC08/FTC09 and the three pattern-omission
sheets were re-inspected: the missing numerical statements really are absent.
All thirty release SVGs are byte-identical to the reviewed G1 drawings, and all
nine omission SVGs/PNGs match the previously inspected envelope/band/pattern
variants. Evidence: `target/g1-fix-quality`, `target/g1-fix-evidence`,
`svg-comparison.log` and `omission-comparison.log` under `target/g1-fix-checks`.

## Stage G G2: plate and wall thickness (2026-10-11)

Ported **ISSUE-1373-a**, **LINT-plate_coverage-thickness**,
**LINT-plate_coverage-exclusions** and **LINT-code-plate_requirement_state**.
The native plate inventory now has ordinary, stable
`d:f:plate:<n>/thickness.length` items. The default follows Python's thin-profile
planner: z left of front, y above side, x below front. Spec omission, tolerance,
view and placement edits use the existing item machinery. Equal raw thicknesses
of one provider body share a counted statement; distinct and unknown bodies do
not join. Printed alternative measurements and physically proven exclusions
cover requirements, while missing, deliberately suppressed and unverifiable
requirements remain distinct lint outcomes. Lint reads Analysis and consumer
annotation provenance, never the Spec or Plan. Zero-ink suppression metadata
states no measured value. Reporting severities are unchanged.

The planner reserves its actual lanes and short-span corner ink. An omitted
counted wall promoted on another axis keeps a strip parallel to its own interval,
rather than copying an orthogonal owner's strip and collapsing its span. Covered
items carry one stable owner ID; all printed dependencies are separately listed.
The general sharing path does not merge plate thicknesses. Default demand avoids
computing plate ownership for inventories without plate wants, or counted labels
for inventories whose plate wants are all covered.

Python's `_thin_profiles.py`, `plate_correspondence.py` and
`detect.py::_append_prismatic_features` were read from origin/main, read-only.
Deliberate native single-axis coverage, body-local counted thickness and the
short-Y plan fallback have verdicts and measured evidence in
`tests/fixtures/known_dimensions.json`. The exact comparison ledgers retain the
remaining FTC-06 crossing and fourteen band overruns. Captured m1000 newly has
four overruns rather than three: its seat callout overruns the front band by
2.1 mm. That residual remains **rust-wrong**, with no changed severity or waived
finding. It is separate from the native quality gate below.

- `src/dims/plates.rs`, `src/dims/mod.rs`, `src/dims/placement.rs`;
  `src/lint/plates.rs`, `src/lint/mod.rs`, `src/layout.rs`, `src/model.rs`.
- `tests/dims_plates.rs`, `tests/plate_coverage.rs`, `tests/dimensions.rs`,
  `tests/issue_regressions.rs`, `tests/issue_trust.rs`, `tests/page_escalation.rs`,
  `tests/placement.rs`; `tests/fixtures/known_dimensions.json`,
  `tests/fixtures/known_leaders.json`, `tests/fixtures/known_placement.json`.
- `tools/check_plates.py`; `docs/model.md`, `docs/next-session.md`, this status file.

No blends, catalogue YAML, quality.py completeness work, or reporting severities
were changed. No commit or push was made.

### Regression evidence

The three new corpus regressions use **10007, 10038 and 10049**. They check one
claimed statement per slab, actual arrow-tip span at view scale, rendered lint,
authored suppression, a bare pin's unchanged geometry, a Spec view move and
repeatable serialization. 10038 includes its native **10.091 mm** wall, printed
**10.1** under the existing one-decimal format.

The six new consumer-coverage tests check actual printed nominal values, counts
and tolerance false credits, malformed intervals, same-interval overall credit,
physical ladder endpoints and both printed dependencies, polygon support/body
scope, exact slot-web replay (including duplicate member rejection), and complete
opposite-channel-wall dependencies. They also check disconnected/unknown bodies,
profile body keys and matching/distinct/null body keys in public planning.

**All nine new tests failed on the pre-G2 implementation**: 0/3 and 0/6 passed,
with real assertion failures, not compile failures
(`target/g2-evidence/all-new-tests-before.log`). The strengthened open-channel
regression also failed before (`channel-test-before.log`); it now requires the
wall's statement and proves removing the thickness claims produces missing-wall
and missing-channel findings. The independent `tools/check_plates.py` fails on
the original quality plans (`before-plate-check.log`) and passes the finished
plans. Existing identity, typed-state/prose, placement, tolerance and comparison
gates remain exercised; expected obsolete missing-plate outcomes are replaced
with thickness assertions, not relaxed gates.

### Sheet audit

The release renderer produced all thirty SVGs through the requested quality
pipeline. Every changed sheet, plus unchanged 10047, was rasterized by resvg at
2400 pixels and inspected individually and on contact sheets
(`target/g2-visual-final/`). The final thirty SVGs are byte-identical to the
reviewed raster inputs, verified against that directory's SHA manifest. Final
re-rasterization produces eleven byte-identical PNGs. Resvg warning counts do
not increase: FTC-08's path-dashing warnings fall from eight to seven; all other
reviewed-sheet warnings are unchanged (`final-raster-warnings.json`).

| Part | Native thickness evidence after G2 | View |
|---|---|---|
| 1000 | 4.219, printed 4.2 | front below |
| 10007 | 11.683 and 3.890, printed 11.7 and 3.9 | front left and below |
| 10033 | 8.338, printed 8.3 | side above |
| 10038 | 4.448 and 10.091, printed 4.4 and 10.1 | front left and side above |
| 10047 | [23.144,31.800] interval, derived from printed 23.1 shoulder and 31.8 overall | side below |
| 10049 | 4.519, printed 4.5 | front below |
| 10060 | 14.315, printed 14.3 | side above |
| FTC-07 | four equal 9.297 walls: 4× 9.3; Y back wall 5.08: 5.1 | front below; plan left |

Across the eight requested parts, **13 slabs have one direct claimed statement
and one is covered by its two printed dependencies** (ten distinct thickness
statements). 10047 has **no separate 8.7 label**: retaining Python's proven
single-axis ladder alternative avoids a closed chain. This interpretation of
'stated once' is explicitly overturnable, rather than represented as a directly
printed thickness. In multi-axis base-and-wall parts, explicit thickness takes
priority over a ladder subtraction.

Other changed sheets are FTC-06 (2× 31.8, both body-local slabs), FTC-08
(4× 6.3 walls and 3.4 top) and GRM-03 (2.5 head thickness). GRM-03's separate
0.5 and 2 turned bands do not alone state the aggregate interval. The added ink
uses profile views with witnesses at the actual faces. Existing crowded ladders,
isometric hidden ink and other inherited drafting defects are not claimed solved.

Overturnable choices are in `docs/next-session.md`: native single-axis slabs;
raw-equal, same-body counts; three-decimal interval match tolerance; physical
ownership requirements; printed ladder/opposite-wall dependencies; short-Y
plan fallback (explicit Spec views prevail); and exact plate corner demand.
Aggregate alternative records without proof of one solid keep explicit thickness.

## G3 — equal-radius blends and typed general notes (2026-10-11)

> Superseded by the G3 fix round below: the original 247 → 10 included unsupported note credit.

Blend requirements fall from 247 unstated radii on fourteen parts to 10. CGB245
(82 → 0), CTC-01 (31 → 0), and the small string_post (2 → 0) meet the requested
regressions. Five or more valid blend occurrences use their most frequent repeated
displayed radius as a general note; other radii retain individual/count leaders or
compact located notes. The general note is scoped explicitly when an exception is
refused/hidden, and withdrawn if an exception cannot be drawn. All coverage credit
comes from exact identities on drawn annotations, never from prose.

The same-face ownership predicate is shared by planning and lint. It replaces only
the duplicate boss diameter and preserves the independent boss height. Automatic
notes have stable Spec/MCP omit/place IDs; authored tables keep their original
layout and serialization. Existing view and leader placement stays stable.

Rules ported in this stream: ISSUE-1433-a, ARCH0015-1 (blend radius grouping),
ISSUE-1450-a (diameter ownership), and ISSUE-1351-a /
LINT-_registry-structured_note_satisfaction (automatic blend-note authority only).
The general authored per-feature satisfaction API remains unported. Python
origin/main reference evidence and explicit verdicts are recorded in
`tests/fixtures/known_blends.json`; no rule catalogue YAML is changed.

Ten requirements remain explicitly refused because no principal view has a proven
attachment on its own trimmed blend surface: CGB207 (1), CTC-02 (3), CTC-03 (2),
and FTC-07 (4). Their scoped notes say `OTHER BLEND RADII UNSTATED`; the lint
remains visible at its original severity. The five-occurrence threshold and
reference-site exception presentation are overturnable judgements in
`docs/next-session.md`. Located notes use one representative point for each
radius group, with reference X/Y/Z offsets from the envelope's left/front/bottom;
whether that is the preferred manufacturing presentation remains undetermined.
