## Stage G G2 review fix round — plate views and open chains (2026-10-11–12)

The review's major findings were correct. 1000's 4.2 wall now uses the plan
profile's solid edges beside the U-slot, and 10038's 4.4 flange uses the exposed
L profile in side. Analysis retains optional straight outer boundary edges;
the planner requires two overlapping thickness edges in a facing boundary
profile of the isolated solid. Body bounds are transformed through the provider
frame before comparison. Explicit Spec view requests still prevail; absent
profile evidence retains Python's fixed axis-map fallback. The witnesses attach
to the proven edge endpoints, rather than an envelope corner with absent geometry.

10007 no longer prints redundant 3.9 or 11.7 labels: retained 28.0/31.9 and
2.3/14.0 respectively convey those intervals. The 2.3 is a through-step baseline
leg with body-local proof; every valid ladder/leg alternative is kept until
actual printed owners are selected. Omitting either owner restores the explicit
thickness. This applies the prior 10047 policy consistently, rather than making
a multi-axis exception. A second, inverse base-slab ownership rule keeps an
explicit top-flange thickness and overall height, covering its underside ladder
station: 10038's 7.2 and FTC-06's 215.9 disappear, removing 7.2+4.4=11.6 and
215.9+31.8=247.7 chains. Stations borrowed to convey another plate stay retained.
Stable Covered IDs and both dependencies remain available for inspection/editing.

Front-left lanes now reserve the front's band independently of plan-left.
Thickness outside-text corners reserve room above the aligned front/side row;
plan-above thickness reserves its actual shared linear lanes. Captured m1000's
new seat-diameter band overrun is fixed: four band findings become the original
three, with no seat-diameter finding.

The saved pre-fix release binary fails all three review probes: 1000 chooses
front instead of plan, 10038 chooses front instead of side, and 10007 retains
both chain-closing labels. The extended check_plates.py also fails against the
old G2 quality output. Tests add view, coverage-dependency, missing/other-body
leg proof, alternative-owner, top-slab restoration and exact seat-overrun checks.
Existing captured-step and omission regressions verify the new characteristic
size ownership with actual owners and restored datum spans. All unrelated view
and ink identity assertions, quality predicates and exact differences checks
remain. No rule YAML, blend code or severity change was made.

Python origin/main `_thin_profiles.register_plate_thickness` was read as the
reference. Its fixed axis map and separate underside rung differ deliberately;
known_dimensions records exposed-profile and closed-chain evidence. The remaining
captured FTC-06 reciprocal leader crossing is explicitly rust-wrong (one to two,
restoring the pre-G2 crossing when the front thickness obstacle moves to side).
Its fourteen existing band findings are unchanged, and native FTC-06 quality
is unchanged. The rejected FTC-07 A3 1:1 recovery alternative still has sixteen
items over a view, versus fourteen before G2, when starting at A3 1:5. It remains
rejected; the final A2 1:2 sheet and its quality are unchanged. Broader alternative
lane packing is deferred, with the required three thicknesses kept. These are
recorded as overturnable scope judgements in next-session.md.

Visual audit: release SVGs rasterised at 2400 px with resvg, individually inspected
for the eight brief parts, native FTC-06/FTC-08 and captured m1000/FTC-06. Thickness
witnesses meet solid edges in the changed views; the redundant station labels
are absent; every reviewed plate has one explicit statement or retained drawn
owners. The final native/captured SVG hash manifests are under target/g2-fix-visual.

Rules retained: ISSUE-1373-a, LINT-plate_coverage-thickness,
LINT-plate_coverage-exclusions, LINT-code-plate_requirement_state.

Verification note: an initial full run encountered an unrelated TCP
ConnectionReset at tests/stateless.rs:103 while reading a transport response.
The exact test passed immediately in isolation; neither transport code nor its
assertions were changed. The complete required suite was rerun afterward.

The editability regression's promoted-owner lookup now reads every exact ID in
coverage's dependency list as well as its first owner. This verifies restoration
when the overall height is omitted, preserving exact survivor sets, promotion
and omission counts, and independently authored items. Its twelve tests pass.

Final verification: fmt check and strict all-target release clippy pass;
corpus-required release tests **678/678 in 73 result groups, zero failed or
ignored**. Release build passes. Final quality compares 30 parts / 300 cells
against the preceding G2 table: every cell unchanged, no part worse. Stage F's
ink 278 and through-feature 212 remain improved at 273 and 211. The final
`--require-go` seed 1/2/3 × N 1/3/8 grid is 270/270 GO, zero off-sheet; scripted
MCP is 7/7 (16 calls). Python quality tests are 20/20 and Node client tests 8/8.
The eight-part plate audit script passes. All ten reviewed native SVGs match
the final quality render hashes; both captured SVGs match their reviewed hashes.
Twelve relevant sheets were individually inspected at 2400 px, including every
native sheet changed from the original G2 output. Grid and MCP evidence use
exactly the final release binary and grid baseline equals final native quality.
Binary SHA-256: `7949e8c721f7d2c7691259e4413a690365f0ff95ace180b60c77ac9b604d7d7b`. Evidence: `target/g2-fix-evidence/final6-*.log`,
`final-verification.json`, `final-pin-grid-summary.json`, `final-mcp-eval.json`,
`final-plates.log`, `target/g2-fix-quality/` and `target/g2-fix-visual/`.
No commit or push was made.

## Stage G G2 review round 3: thickness witnesses leave through the silhouette (2026-10-12)

Major finding fixed. On 10033 (8.3 in plan) and 10049 (4.5 in front), the
thickness witnesses were anchored where the two solid plate edges overlap, but the
strip came from Python's fixed axis map. The extension lines therefore started
mid-view and ran across the part. 10038's 10.1, above the side, had the same fault.
`plates::prefer_readable` now anchors at the end of the overlap that lies on the
view's silhouette. `placement::lanes` puts the dimension in the strip on that
side. New plate bands (front above, plan right or below, side left) are reserved
in `Lanes` and `Strips::reserve`. If no view has a pair that reaches the
silhouette, the pair nearest it is used, provided it is within 3 mm on the page.
That keeps FTC-06's counted 2× 31.8 right of the side, where its witnesses run
over the middle section's object lines. Otherwise the fixed-map fallback is
unchanged. A horizontal side thickness below the view now also reserves the
side's left gap. Without that, 10038 had a new 4.7 mm band overrun, ink 1 → 2.

New sheets: 10049's 4.5 is above the front, 10038's 10.1 is below the side inside
29.7, and 10033's 8.3 is above the side. The plan foot's lower edge ends at the
C3.4 chamfer, so it cannot be anchored at the plan silhouette. Only these three
sheets differ from `target/g2-fix-quality`. Each was rasterised and inspected:
every witness now starts at the silhouette edge facing its dimension line.
Residual on 10033: the side's R6.1 callout sits between the 8.3 witnesses, and
its shoulder ends on the right witness. That is a touch, not a counted crossing.
Counting lines within LABEL_PAD of a label would move it, but it changes leader
placement across the corpus, so it is left to the leader work.

Regression: `tests/dims_plates.rs` checks that each thickness witness starts at
the view outline edge facing its dimension line, allowing the 1 mm gap. It runs
on 1000, 10033 (new), 10038, 10049 and 10060 (new). The pre-fix source fails it
on 10033, 10049 and 10038.

The minor review points are recorded in `docs/next-session.md`. The coverage
policy for derived-only thicknesses (10007, 10047, FTC-07 plates 2 and 5) is
stated there as an open judgement for the brief owner, not as acceptance. The
FTC-07 rejected-alternative count (16 against 14) is unchanged. The HTTP MCP
ConnectionReset flake is fixed: the server now half-closes and drains an unread
request body before closing.

Verification: cargo fmt --check passes, and strict all-target release clippy is
clean. With the corpus required, the release suite passes 680/680 in 73 result
groups, with none failed or ignored. Release quality on the 30 parts matches the
preceding table cell for cell: no part worse, ink 273, through-feature 211.
`check_quality_table.py` passes. The `--require-go` pin grid (seeds 1/2/3 × N
1/3/8) is 270/270 GO, and the scripted MCP evaluation is 7/7. Binary SHA-256 is
`dd3f1c4ba2bfdab126545900b380af57fcf8243467421c719cb9204f2de0b123`.

Rules ported (unchanged): ISSUE-1373-a, LINT-plate_coverage-thickness,
LINT-plate_coverage-exclusions, LINT-code-plate_requirement_state.

## Stage G integration (2026-10-12)

### What merged

Merged into `main` from base `b999b13`, in order, each with `--no-ff`:

| Stream | Branch (commits) | Implemented by | Review rounds |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| G4 rules catalogue from the 2026-10-11 assessment | `cxg/g4-catalogue` (49b81a6) | Claude (no Codex) | none (data only; YAML loads, named tests exist) |
| G1 honest completeness and lint ledgers | `cxg/g1-honest` (339de70, f0a53a9) | Codex (98 min + 95 min fix) | 2: round 1 one blocker (12 false pattern-location warnings on CTC-02/CTC-05) and three minor; fixed; round 2 passed with minor notes |
| G2 plate and wall thickness | `cxg/g2-plates` (f0dbbae, b42a307, d322411) | Codex (177 min + 101 min fix), then Claude (fix 2) | 3: round 1 two major and three minor; round 2 one major (thickness witnesses crossing the part on 10033/10049) and three minor; Claude takeover fixed it; round 3 passed with minor notes |
| G5 title block | `cxg/g5-titleblock` (014e524) | Codex (94 min) | 1: passed with minor notes |

Not merged, because their reviews did not reach an acceptable state: G3 blends
(`g3-blends`), G6 centre lines (`g6-centrelines`) and G7b recesses (`g7b-recesses`).

Conflicts: `docs/next-session.md` and this file (append-only; both sides kept in merge
order), `docs/mcp.md` (G1's completeness paragraph and G5's title-block paragraph both
kept), `src/model.rs` (G2's authored-plate-omission annotations and G5's projection-symbol
ink are independent; both kept), and `tests/model.rs` / `tests/serve.rs`, where a
default Spec now raises both G1's `unclaimed_geometry` and G5's
`general_tolerance_missing`: the assertions now require both, with G1's full
`unclaimed_geometry` record and G5's fix op unchanged. One semantic conflict surfaced in
the merged suite: `tests/completeness.rs::multi_solid_completeness_findings_are_info`
asserted `status: clear` under a default Spec, which G5's tolerance warning now turns
into `needs_attention`. The test now states a general tolerance so it still isolates
G1's claim (multi-solid completeness findings are informational); no assertion was
removed. Only G1 changed the MCP schemas; `tools/generate_mcp_schemas.py` regenerates
them with no diff. No known-difference record was changed by two streams.
`tools/quality.py` keeps every column (G1 added the three completeness columns; G2 and
G5 added none).

Dependency: quiddity-rust release `v0.1.0-alpha.1` (4583c740), unchanged.

## Stage H / issue #2: CI acceptance checks (2026-10-11)

Verified the reviewer’s claim against Stage F head `b999b13`: the CI workflow
still omitted the quality, repeated-span, pin, MCP and instrument gates. The
review’s 13 Python / 6 Node successes and missing-release-binary HTTP failure
are historical: current local runs pass **20 Python / 8 Node**, including HTTP.

Added three acceptance matrix jobs in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, all calling
`tools/ci_acceptance.py`. They reuse the existing build job’s nextest-built
release executable and pinned corpus checkout through a tar artifact, with
no second compilation. All 42 local corpus inputs were independently compared
to the Git blobs at `6d544e060cd0eeb4442d2196e5df757b5a15ea55`: byte-identical.
The local runner records hashes and timings, checks complete fixture/result
sets, generates the same-binary pin baseline, and enforces `--require-go`.
Its committed `tools/ci-quality-baseline.json` protects all ten Stage F columns
for every one of the thirty parts, without the Markdown checker’s missing-column
compatibility exception. The machine baseline is derived from the merged Stage F
table and a fresh run; **all 300 cells are identical**. No planner, layout or
renderer source, drawing policy, criterion or package dependency changed.

`tools/test_ci_acceptance.py` adds **10 tests**: equality/improvement, each
regressed/missing quality column, missing/error parts, all-64/zero survey,
forged pin GO/threshold/same-run baseline, incomplete pin samples, seven
unique successful MCP tasks, CI entry point/always-upload wiring, required
MCP judge documents/PNGs, and missing fixture rejection. Each test failed
against an archive of the original HEAD with only the new tests copied in
(**10 failures**, `target/h2-before-tests.log`); all ten pass after the change.
The real quality report was also deliberately changed from 1000 ink **1 to 2**;
the unchanged bar rejected it with **exit 1** (`deliberate-rejection.log`).

`tools/mcp-eval/run-scripted.mjs` saves 150 dpi sheets alongside judge
documents when requested. All seven PNGs were verified and the pinned plate
was visually inspected for readability. A same-binary run without judge
artifacts has **identical task call/byte/token records** to the artifact run.
MCP is still a scripted instrument, with unchanged S0 conditions. It passes
**7/7**, **16 calls**, **5,674 estimated tool tokens**. Stage F recorded 5,669;
the estimate includes absolute input paths and can vary between worktrees.
Path length is the inferred explanation for this small difference; the
artifact ablation directly establishes that judge renders add no measured
calls or tokens.

## Stage H / issue #2 fix round: hosted acceptance limits (2026-10-11)

The reviewer’s two blockers hold in the earlier committed worktree: one
quality/pin job scheduled nine cells under a 30-minute job timeout, and the
survey inherited a 600 s render/plan timeout plus a 1500 s runner-step timeout.
The earlier “conservative ceiling” claim was wrong. Hosted evidence provided
by the reviewer (run 38171632086) measures 342 s for quality and ~432 s per
pin cell; the survey timed out rendering threaded_connector_109. No hosted
run was dispatched during this fix round.

The workflow now has nine quality/pin shards (one seed 1/2/3 × N 1/3/8 cell
each), plus survey and MCP. Each shard generates and validates its own fresh
30-part baseline with the reused release executable, then enforces
`--require-go` on all thirty pin runs. This preserves the entire 270-run grid
without a cross-job baseline dependency. Check/artifact labels are unique,
fail-fast remains false, and evidence uploads still use always(). Hosted
pin-shard wall time projects to 774 s (~13 min); each has a 30-minute limit.

The complete 64-part survey keeps its four workers and now passes a 3600 s
per-command timeout, with a 6600 s runner-step limit and a 120-minute job limit.
The previous 745 s local survey projects to ~68 min at the observed ~5.5x
hosted slowdown. These are bounded, overturnable timing choices documented in
docs/next-session.md; further hosted measurements can revise them. This choice
avoids changing the survey population or drawing calculations, but retains a
long hosted survey job. MCP has a 10-minute limit (reviewer measured 25 s).

TimeoutExpired now becomes a per-part error row, so a timed-out part still
fails and baseline.json preserves all 64 survey results. The runner checks
subprocess exit status before validating result files: failures now identify
“survey exited 1; see survey.log” instead of hiding the cause behind ENOENT.
Five added regression tests cover matrix completeness/same-baseline invocation,
hosted timeout budgets, render and plan timeout evidence, complete survey error
reports, and subprocess failure reporting. All five fail against the earlier
committed code with the new tests copied in; all fifteen acceptance tests pass
with the corpus present. No existing acceptance threshold was relaxed. No
Rust, planner, layout, renderer, baseline or dependency changed.

Final end-to-end local verification passes (`target/h2-fix-acceptance/summary.json`):
30 parts / 300 quality cells equal to the committed Stage F baseline, all
270/270 pin runs GO, 64/64 survey parts with zero repeated spans, and all
seven MCP tasks passing. The real deliberate 1000 ink 1→2 regression is still
rejected with exit 1. Pin ink totals equal the earlier nine-cell table exactly.
Required fmt, Python quality tests (20/20), release build and Node client tests
(8/8) pass; the full runner also passes all fifteen acceptance tests. Cargo
commands were serial with jobs/threads set to eight. Workflow YAML parses into
11 unique acceptance matrix entries (Ruby’s standard YAML parser); actionlint
is not installed, so no actionlint run is claimed.

|---|---:|

| quality-pins | 671.680 |

| Full sequential runner | 1169.254 |

## Stage H1: plan-time hole tables, with fix rounds 1 and 2 (2026-10-12)

Branch `cxg/h-tables`, on top of Stage H integration (`0fc1c03`). Hole tables are now
chosen at plan time, per view, when the hole callouts and location ladders would be
illegible. The trigger is a legibility measure (at least 6 holes, plus crowded
ordinates, more than 8 location statements, or a leader crossing), not a list of parts.
The 16-hole rescue stays as a backstop. The Spec setting `hole_table` takes
`auto|always|never`. Columns are `TAG | ø / DEPTH / THREAD | X | Y | Z OUT / DIR`,
all in the view's frame and measured from the theoretical datum corner. Bolt circles
of 5 or more equal holes get an in-table PCD with each actual angle.

Fix round 2 (this entry) resolves the two major review findings:

- **Tags name their own hole.** On CTC-02, 27 of 72 tags were as close to another
  scheduled hole as to their own, and 30 were more than 12 mm away. A tag now sits
  beside its hole only where it is plainly nearer that hole. Otherwise it gets a
  short leader from the hole's rim. The reviewer's check, extended to leadered tags
  (the leader tip must be on its own rim and more than 1 mm from any other), finds
  no ambiguous or distant tag on any of the 11 sheets that have a schedule
  (CTC-02 59 of 72 tags leadered, CTC-04 4/44, CTC-05 8/18, FTC-06 5/12).
- **Entry column in the view frame** (`Z OUT / DIR`, Z out of the page). Lint checks
  the cell in that frame.

Quality after (`tools/quality.py`, 30 parts), against the CI baseline (Stage G merged
after table). No cell is worse; the changed cells are:

| part | repeated_label | ink | line_through_feature |
|---|---|---|---|
| cgb207 | 0 → 0 | 7 → 2 | 4 → 4 |
| cgb245 | 1 → 1 | 6 → 1 | 11 → 3 |
| nist_ctc_02_asme1_rc | 1 → 0 | 36 → 2 | 36 → 16 |
| nist_ctc_03_asme1_rc | 0 → 0 | 24 → 6 | 8 → 4 |
| nist_ctc_04_asme1_rd | 5 → 2 | 54 → 3 | 62 → 36 |

The other seven columns are unchanged on every part, including hidden_on_visible
(2263). `tools/ci-quality-baseline.json` is refreshed to these values. All 300 cells

Verification (Cargo serial, 8 jobs/threads): `cargo fmt --check` and

## Stage H / H3 (G13): text on its span with clear lane promotion (2026-10-12)

Implemented ISSUE-1334-a, ISSUE-1333-a and ADR2-10's bounded linear-label solve.
The completed leader/GD&T assignment is retained; frames and datum leaders are
immutable obstacles. Try text between its own witnesses, or adjacent to an arrow
when too short; if this cannot clear ink, try outward paper lanes within the
existing footprint. Candidates preserve witnesses and measurements, cannot add
contact or band-overrun items, and cannot share overlapping measured spans on a
dimension line. The full inventory preserves omission locality, while text edits
fix unchanged peers on canonical witnesses. Unresolved ink remains reported.

`quality.py` independently counts `text_off_span` and stores the IDs. It counts
the old 5.7 mm outside gap, or a foreign extension between the text and span.
The metric introduces no MCP problem rows. Thresholds and bounds are overturnable
in `docs/next-session.md`; Python-reference translations have verdicts/evidence
in `tests/fixtures/known_label_lanes.json`. The rule YAML files are untouched.

Visual review rejected the first implementation's eight new shared-span pairs
across five parts, although its ink total was 265. The final guard keeps the
existing FTC-10 57 mm callout crossings visible instead of moving that row onto
the 10 mm step-height lane. The accepted final ink total is 268. CTC-03 retains
its first clean A3 at 1:5 instead of escalating to A2 at the same scale; its
measurement tuples are unchanged, ink 24→20 and feature crossings 8→5.

All thirty native sheets and STC-09 were rendered with the final release binary
and rasterised with `resvg -w 2400 --background white`. All thirty were reviewed,
then the five sheets changed by the shared-span guard were re-reviewed. STC-09's
28 GD&T objects (including glyphs, leaders, sites, state and basis), sheet and
views are exactly equal to the original Plan. `gdt_band.rs` and all STC-09
fixtures stay untouched. The independent acceptance script rejects unchanged
output and checks all measurements, every per-part metric, new shared spans
and exact STC-09 equality.

New regressions: one native test (1000's 6.9/8.9/10.9 and 10020's 4.5/1.2), one
synthetic lane test (datum obstruction, occupied far lane, fixed witnesses/pins,
idempotence), and five Python metric tests. Both Rust tests and all five Python
tests fail before the change. The synthetic proof delegates the same ink inputs
to the original `placement::settle_fixed`: y remains 30 rather than 51. The
original source with the native test fails on 1000's 6.9. The acceptance script
also fails on the unchanged before output, and its shared-span guard rejects
the first implementation. Existing Rust tests are unchanged: no weakened
thresholds, baseline exceptions or part-specific solver cases. Collision
recomputation is limited to dimension/GD&T subjects, retaining title-block
off-sheet findings; the unchanged title tests caught and verify that fix.

## Stage H / H3 review fix: bounded promotion and readable clearance (2026-10-12)

The earlier completion claim is superseded. The reviewer correctly found that
GD&T equality and a falling ink count could coexist with a worse drawing:
STC-09's 38.1 slot size jumped 168 mm across the location tower, and many labels
were nudged just 0.13–0.50 mm off leaders. Those are rejected repairs.

The search now traverses the inventory once, with finite span/obstacle boundary
candidates and allocation-free contact masks. It tries the next outward lane,
skipping at most one occupied dimension rung, with an absolute 14 mm bound.
A fixed-ink obstruction alone cannot justify the skip. Cleared text/line
contacts need 1 paper mm, checked per stroke. Existing proximity of an unchanged
witness is distinguished from the stroke actually being cleared. CTC-03 needs
the one-rung skip: the first lane holds 447.3 grid pitch; the 108.0 slot size
uses the next available lane and retains the clearer A3 sheet. A literal 7 mm
cap instead re-escalated it to A2 (24 ink, 8 feature crossings).

New checks cover the blocked datum/tower, one occupied rung, visible clearance,
STC-09's neighbouring slot sizes and witness length, and the missing 10007 14.0
and string_post 1.1 examples. The CI quality/pin runner now runs the locality
script and regenerates the AP242 Plan from the committed gzip. The enhanced
before/after checker regenerates both protected Plans from supplied binaries,
checks all protected dimension displacements and witness origins, and checks
newly cleared STC-09 label/stroke contacts for 1 mm clearance. It rejects the
reviewed H3 output for both clearance and locality. Existing GD&T band tests,
STC-09 fixtures, quality thresholds and MCP tasks remain unchanged. The CI
instrument test is strengthened to require the new gate as well as the pin bar.

The metric caveat is material: **151 → 23** in the earlier report includes
**108** original labels exactly at the old default 5.7 mm outside gap. The
strictly-greater interpretation was **43 → 16**. Most of the inclusive fall is
bringing default outside text closer to its own arrow or inside its span;
it does not measure lane-promotion count. The inclusive metric remains named
and documented, without changing its tests or adding MCP noise.

The thirteen cited callout crossings are not thirteen feasible promotion cases:
seven are extension lines, which outward promotion lengthens; six are dimension
lines with blocked/occupied adjacent lanes. The agreed dense-part remedy is
tables/detail sheets, not farther jumps or GD&T rerouting. Those findings stay
visible. Final validation and measurements follow below.


### Final-source validation

- Required **fmt**, **strict all-target release Clippy**, and required-corpus
  **cargo test --release**: **720 passed / 75 groups, 0 failed, 0 ignored**.
  The final full invocation includes all eight session tests and the unchanged
  150 ms latency gate. Earlier noisy/slow session failures are retained in the
  evidence; the scoring mode is a const generic, with no new runtime branch in
  ordinary drawings. No test or threshold was relaxed.
- Release build passes. The ordinary thirty-part quality invocation into an
  existing absolute directory passes **420/420 cells no worse** against the
  saved pre-fix status table: **268 ink, 208 feature crossings, 23 off-span**.
  All thirty native SVGs are byte-identical to the reviewed H3 output.
- The pre-H3 comparison remains **273→268 ink**, **151→23 inclusive off-span**.
  Measurements and shared-span guards pass. Fixed A3 CTC-03 demonstrates the
  genuine **14 mm** promotion, **x=107.5927→121.5927**, with unchanged witnesses
  and measurements; it is independent of the recovered A2/A3 view placement.
- STC-09: all **28 GD&T objects**, sheet and views exactly equal pre-H3. **No
  dimension changes lane** against that baseline. The 38.1 size stays at
  **y=444.9062**, beside 2×25.4, with ~49 mm witnesses instead of ~217 mm.
  Its annotation-ink count is **45→23**, with **22 removed stroke/label contacts
  checked for at least 1 mm**. This is intentionally higher than the rejected
  branch's 10: sub-millimetre apparent fixes are no longer claimed as repaired.
- Isolated alternating-process medians, three pairs, no Cargo process during
  measurement: default **2.745→2.144 s (0.781x)**; tolerance edit
  **4.589→4.861 s (1.059x)**. Both pass the predeclared **1.25x** ceiling,
  compared with the review's default 2.6x slowdown. Repeated Plan bytes match.
  Profiling found expensive footprint-aware scores computed then discarded on
  semantic-PMI drawings that always retain the legacy objective. Skipping those
  unused calculations preserves candidate geometry, legacy scores, assignment
  and full STC-09 Plan bytes; the new equivalence regression passes.
- All **31 sheets** release-rendered and rasterized with resvg at width 2400.
  All were inspected; the only changed sheet against reviewed H3 is STC-09,
  with two high-resolution vector crops confirming the restored neighbouring
  size and readable spacing. Original unresolved crossings remain visible.
- Pin grid, every cell with **--require-go**: **270/270 GO**, zero off-sheet.
  Pinned ink totals by seed 1/2/3 × N 1/3/8:
  **260/253/252, 266/216/220, 264/253/233**, identical to reviewed H3.
- Scripted MCP **7/7**, **16 calls**, **5812** estimated byte-based tool tokens;
  all seven judge document/PNG sets exist. `tasks.json` is unchanged.
  Python quality **28/28**, CI instruments **15/15**, Node client **8/8** pass.

## Stage H / H3 review fix 2: 1 mm from newly approached strokes (2026-10-12)

The reviewer found four moved labels newly within 0.5 mm of another item's
stroke, including STC-09's 63.5 against the perpendicularity frame's leader
and string_post's 5.5 on the hole location's dimension line. Settlement now
rejects a candidate that brings any other item's stroke or label within 1 paper
mm of the moved label (or the moved lines within 1 mm of another label) unless
it was already that close. Outside-arrow dimensions now also try text just past
an arrow tail, after every inside place. See `docs/next-session.md`.

- fmt, strict all-target release Clippy; required-corpus `cargo test --release`:
  **721 passed, 0 failed**. The two new regression checks in
  `tests/label_lanes.rs` fail at 7a060ae.
- Quality: **420 cells no worse** than the previous table; totals **268 ink,
  208 feature crossings, 16 off-span** (inclusive and strict now agree; pre-H3
  151 inclusive / 43 strict). Pre-H3 → now: ink 273 → 268. Measurements, shared
  spans and STC-09 GD&T, sheet and views are unchanged (`check_label_lanes.py`).
- Reviewer's 0.5 mm scan: 4 → 0 labels moved onto new strokes, except three
  nist_ctc_03 items that follow the recovered A2 → A3 sheet change (none at a
  fixed A3 1:5 sheet). STC-09 at 1 mm: 0.
- Random-pin grid, `--require-go`: **270/270 GO**, zero off-sheet. Seed 1 N=8
  first timed out on two renders (600 s) while the machine's load average was
  about 70 from other runs; the rerun at lower load is GO.
- Scripted MCP evaluation: **7/7**, `tools/mcp-eval/tasks.json` unchanged.
- Checked by eye: STC-09 63.5 now inside its span clear of the leader; the
  height tower; string_post 5.5 just past its arrow, clear of the line.

## Stage H6: measured sheet and scale choice (2026-10-12)

The maintainer's rule is implemented in `model::recover` / `sheet_fit::choose`:

- find the smallest valid complete fit, at its largest scale;
- allow at most the next larger sheet, and only at a larger scale;
- choose by drawn-item fill (target band `sheet_fit::FILL_BAND`, 60–85%); below the band,
  the fullest valid pair wins;
- named Spec choices win.

Against a valid first estimate, a pair that adds leaders or witnesses through material is set
aside. If every allowed pair adds them, the first estimate is kept. Results:

- the supplied plate: A3 1:1 at 38.01% fill (was A2 1:1, 17.99%);
- native GRM-03: A3 10:1 at 60.06% (was A4 2:1, 13.94%);
- 10060, 10063 and 10000 stay on A4 1:1;
- assembly_plate_pin and spool_fits stay on A2 1:1;
- STC-09 keeps its first drawing (A1 1:5) and reports a warning.

[H6 sheet policy](h6-sheet-policy.md) has every part's sheet, scale and fill before and after
(30 native, 14 AP242 and the plate), the tests and the judgement calls.

On the native 30, `quality.py` passes all 390 cells against the preceding table, with no part
worse: ink 273→262, material 211→207, hidden-on-visible 2263→2257; other counts unchanged.
On the AP242 14 plus the plate, all 195 cells pass against the archived pre-H6 binary: ink
1612→1593, material 447→446, hidden 1425→1424. The random-pin grid with `--require-go` is
9/9 GO with no new off-sheet items, and the scripted MCP evaluation succeeds on 7/7 tasks. The new ratchet table:

## G3 fix round — radius authority and shared presentation (2026-10-11)

This entry supersedes the preceding G3 result. The review's blocker and major
finding are correct: an exact `satisfies` identity does not locate an arbitrary
radius subset, and the fillet/blend distinction must not create duplicate radius
text on paper. The original 247 → 10 was not a valid completeness result.

Scoped notes and representative `REF SITE XYZ` notes are removed. Unsafe automatic
blend leaders are explicitly refused, and their radii remain missing. A true
`ALL UNSPECIFIED RADII R…` default is drawable only while every different blend or
fillet radius has a real callout. Full drawing lint independently validates that
condition, the printed value, and the exact claimed identities. Authored prose
still grants no authority. Hiding an exception withdraws its dependent default;
it can restore an equal fillet leader that the default had covered, but cannot
move unrelated dimensions. An undrawn automatic block leaves no heading on paper.

Equal blend and fillet radii share presentation. CGB245's R3 default absorbs its
24× R3 fillet leader. Its R4 group uses the established fillet leader with a single
28× count, replacing the parallel 18× and 10× leaders. The recogniser's distinction
no longer appears in the default text. Intrinsic slot-end radii keep their existing
shape grammar. Stable IDs and explicit Spec omission, placement and tolerance
choices remain authoritative; nominal authored complete-set listings preserve
automatic consolidation. Covered measurements enter the covered-item ledger.

Automatic radius blocks now sit 6 mm inside the drawing frame. Duplicate physical
attachment projections are removed at 1e-9 mm before searching routes, without
changing the selected view or distinct projected sites. Defaults with known
undrawn exceptions skip futile note-box placement. The 150 ms session gate stays
unchanged. An isolated post-optimisation CTC-01 run measured median 44.9 ms and
p90/max 148.8 ms; a subsequent complete session target measured max 109.6 ms.
The pre-optimisation focused audit measured median 87.5 ms and max 330.5 ms.
These samples establish an improvement, not immunity to host outliers or equality
to main's latency.

The engraving minor remains narrowly open: recognition has no lettering ownership
signal. CTC-01's safe R4.9/R6.3 and FTC-08's R3.3/R0.5/R4.2 leaders still
land on their logos; other NIST sheets also retain lettering leaders. A radius,
position, shallow-depth or part-name exclusion would risk suppressing real part
features. Coordinate-note logo instances disappear with the rejected fallback.
The conservative choice and its overturnable scope are recorded in
`docs/next-session.md`.

Regression and acceptance evidence:

- `tests/dims_blends.rs`: genuine CTC-01 missing count; CTC-02 refuses unlocated
  R10 credit; CGB245 has no equal-value default/leader duplication; small groups
  retain proven trimmed-surface attachments; hide, move, unplace and identity
  edits retain exact authority. Mutating drawn default text to either scoped or
  XYZ prose restores all 33 affected missing requirements.
- `tests/placement.rs` and `tests/trust_rules.rs`: removing a radius exception withdraws the default
  and restores the exact same fillet label and geometry as hiding that note;
  unrelated sheet, view and dimension placement assertions remain.
- `tests/issue_trust.rs`, `tests/leaders.rs`: mixed-family satisfaction checks the
  feature kind, printed radius and exact identity. Every unsafe undrawn blend
  has an explicit typed refusal and corresponding problem. Other callout families
  retain their existing required-leader policy.
- `tools/check_blend_quality.py`: all 300 cells are compared independently, all
  14 affected parts are reviewed natively, scoped/XYZ notes and duplicate default
  leaders are rejected, frame clearance is checked, and every missing blend has
  an explicit refusal. Running it on the previous committed G3 sheets fails on
  the unlocated cgb207 note (`/tmp/g3fix-red-script.log`).
- The exact captured-recognition own-drawing lint ledger restores CTC-01's
  19 missing blends (native recognition has 23). After projection deduplication,
  string_post has a safe leader on both paths and needs no missing-radius entry.
  The exact assertion remains unchanged.

Rules retained: ISSUE-1433-a, ARCH0015-1 (blend grouping), ISSUE-1450-a,
ISSUE-1351-a and LINT-_registry-structured_note_satisfaction (automatic radius
notes only). `tests/fixtures/known_blends.json` records the deliberate general-note
and dependency differences from Python's separate counted radius jobs, plus the
conservative departure from Python Policy B for obstructed automatic blend
additions. Python's `tests/test_issue_1187_unroutable_leaders.py` retains required
obstructed leaders with diagnostics; this stream withholds unsafe automatic blend
additions to meet its no-new-clutter acceptance, with missing coverage intact.
No rules YAML, reporting severity or quality.py completeness logic is changed.

## G3 carry-over resume — final drawing checks (2026-10-12, Codex)

The named drawing blockers are fixed, but the required all-gates verdict remains
incomplete: NIST session latency still exceeds 150 ms on this heavily loaded
host. The final pin grid, quality comparison and scripted MCP checks pass.

Resumed the committed WIP 1d14760. Formatting and the Session let-chain lint are
fixed. The two failing blend tests exposed inconsistent radius-table formatting:
CTC-02 centre locators were rounded to one decimal although independent lint
required three; FTC-08's R3.175 was displayed as R3.2 but checked as diagnostic
text. Centres now retain up to three decimals and radius cells are checked at
printed precision. A red regression also prevents exponent/leading-dot text from
borrowing a different number. The relief note now has an informational, exact
feature-to-note disposition; hiding it restores unplanned requirements. The
unchanged dimensioned-or-reported trust assertion passes, and note provenance
checks exact printed text, geometric evidence and empty radius claims.

CTC-01 now states all four real R25 centres and places ALL UNSPECIFIED RADII R10;
its lettering radii carry no callouts or fabricated claims. FTC-06 lettering is
also excluded while body radii survive. Radius tables locate every exception on
CTC-01, CTC-02 and FTC-08; a required leader is never withdrawn for routing cost.
Geometry thresholds and the model-governing relief note remain conservative,
overturnable drafting policy, documented in docs/next-session.md and the
known-blends verdicts. There is no production part-name filter.

The blend acceptance script reports 247 original requirements -> 19 missing:
CGB207 4, CTC-03 7, FTC-07 8. Seven exception occurrences lack a principal view
with proven trimmed-surface attachment and prevent twelve dependent default
members (3/5/4). All have explicit refusals; none are routing withdrawals. These
need further attachment/view evidence, potentially detail views or sections.

Final-release quality: no part worse in all 300 published cells against the
previous complete table, and all 390 comparable cells against saved G3 pre-change
plans. Ink/line-through are 273/199, versus latest status 278/212 and saved G3
273/211. The distinction avoids attributing rebased-main changes to this resume.
All thirteen changed sheets against stage-hh/before were rasterised at 2400 px
and inspected, including CTC-01, FTC-06 and CGB245. Final SVGs and freshly rendered
PNGs match the inspected hashes. The explicit relief note plus every-occurrence
radius table is accepted as a model-governed drafting requirement. Existing dense
hole ladders, small views and section defects remain outside this stream.

Acceptance remains incomplete because the latency gate fails. Final fmt and strict release
clippy pass; the release build passes; final scripted MCP is 7/7 in 16 calls with
unchanged tasks and a fresh cache. The final required-corpus complete test run
reached all 75 result groups: 727 passed, one failed, zero ignored. Its sole failure was
NIST session p90 212.826 ms against the unchanged 150 ms gate. The immediately
preceding complete source version passed 728/0/0 before the lexical guard; other
hardening repeats failed plate/NIST latency. Host load subsequently reached 78.55
on 18 logical CPUs, evidence of contention but not proof or a gate exemption.
The final random-pin grid with --require-go passes all nine configurations,

recorded in docs/next-session.md. Existing Stage F drafting defects are carried

## Stage G / G6 review fix round (2026-10-11)

The review correctly found that the first attempt cleared only the finite A–A
stroke: string_post's `2.4 A/F` then touched the left section letter, while
CTC-05's `527.05 A/F` retained a right-letter collision. Both letters now enter
fixed ink with the actual rendering font's glyph boxes, together with the
arrowhead and wing outlines from the renderer's `section::cutting_plane` function.
No new furniture style or dimensional-family rule is introduced.

CTC-05 also exposed the previous fallback's all-or-nothing restore: the joint
solve could buy furniture clearance with an ordinary pair crossing, triggering
restoration of the colliding original assignment. The small obstacle-hook fix
starts from that original assignment and repairs only its hard furniture
contacts, accepting existing routes within the ordinary-ink floor against all
currently selected routes. It adds bounded furniture-only alternatives to the existing inventories and
removes fallback remeasurement. This is the necessary
shared-file merge point in `dims/leaders.rs`; dimensional content, linear lane
planning and G2/G3 feature-routing rules are unchanged.

The review's metric criticism is also correct. Zero `label_centerline_overlap`
findings and unchanged ink 278 did not measure leaders running along A–A or
labels touching section letters. Structural drawing review now reports
`leader_centerline_overlap` (over 0.5 mm of shared finite stroke) and
`label_section_furniture_overlap` (actual label/letter or arrow-box contact beyond
numerical roundoff). Both contribute to quality ink. Transverse crossings and
remote collinear extensions are independently tested as clear.

`g6_section_contacts.json` preserves the real pre-G6 string_post leaders and
first-G6 string_post/CTC-05 callout geometry. The regression requires detection
of both original A–A leader contacts and both later letter contacts, while the
fixed drawings must be clear. The strengthened original section test asserts
clearance from the rendered letter and arrow boxes. Red evidence:
`target/g6-fix/red-letter.log` fails on the original letter collision;
`red-structural.log` fails to detect the captured original leaders with the new
lint hook disabled; `captured.log` fails on CTC-05 before fallback repair.
No original drafting assertion or threshold was relaxed.

The actual-label-only centreline policy is retained, with concrete evidence:
string_post's section hatch box is
`[182.00003333342593,81.1,193.99996666657407,107.6]`. The new regression proves
Python's whole-ink fallback would count that unchanged hatch as text on its
axis. The module documentation now states actual labels only. The independent
Python capture comparison retains all twelve original ported-code assertions
and additionally checks all seventeen newly detected collinear contacts on
eight saved Python sheets, named in `g6_python_fixed_ink.json`. These deliberate
differences and the now-cleared FTC-06 hole:5 leader/section-letter defect are
recorded in `known_centrelines.json`; the exhaustive stale structural entry is
removed without changing the test's inventory-equality assertion.

The earlier implementation was committed by the driver as `0dbba2f`; the agent
made no commit or push during this fix. Centre/hidden precedence is paint order,
so chain ink may remain in hidden-dash gaps. Recognised geometry still supplies
furniture; the seven previously listed recognition gaps, including 10038, remain
explicit overturnable judgements in `docs/next-session.md`.


Final routing refinement (2026-10-12): the expanded review found six previously
uncounted centre-stroke contacts on CTC-02, CTC-04 and FTC-10. The obstacle hook
adds bounded alternatives only around an incumbent blocked by fixed ink: nearby
rim points, shortened shelves with fixed text endpoints and the opposite outward
side. A bounded local descent can use room freed elsewhere while preserving the
whole ordinary-ink floor and every callout's feature-crossing floor. Ordinary
candidate generation and the joint assignment/search are unchanged; no rule
catalogue YAML, dimension value, family planner or title block is changed.

FTC-10 hole:17's nearby round-rim attachment is a deliberate, overturnable
exception to the nearest radial attachment: shifting toward the old label would
intersect four other features. The accepted rim point keeps that label and elbow,
clears the centre stroke and preserves zero feature crossings. The regression
checks its actual radius and outward departure, alongside zero fixed-ink contacts
on all three sheets. The shared near-line predicate is retained for ordinary ink;
only furniture's hard travel classification now distinguishes collinearity from
a transverse crossing of a short centre mark.

Visual QA also caught the repaired string_post shelf crossing a filled section
arrow. The full arrow/wing boxes now reserve opaque ink through the existing
obstacle channel, and the strengthened test checks segment clearance as well as
label clearance. Final detailed rasters and validation are reported below.

Visual QA of the repaired sheets: `target/g6-fix/quality-4/` was rendered with
the release executable, and all 30 SVGs were rasterised on white using
`resvg -w 2400`. All five contact sheets were inspected, plus full sheets for
string_post, CTC-02, CTC-04, CTC-05, FTC-06, FTC-10, cgb241, cgb207 and GRM-05.
string_post's left label sits below the section letter and its shoulder clears
the complete arrow/wing ink; the right label clears its letter. CTC-05's A/F
label sits clear of its right letter, and the pitch circle passes through ten
radial/tangential crosses. The turned shaft, section and bore axes have their
planned overruns. The existing crowded NIST dimension stacks and seven feature
recognition gaps remain visible; no new section-letter or arrow collision was
accepted. The final validation render is compared byte-for-byte below.

Final validation (2026-10-12): `cargo fmt --check`, strict all-targets release
clippy, the corpus-required release suite (**676 passed, zero failed or ignored**)
and release build all pass. Python quality tests: **21/21**; Node MCP client
tests: **8/8**. The scripted stateless HTTP MCP runner succeeds on **7/7 tasks**.
All 30 final SVGs are byte-identical to the inspected `quality-4` sheets and were
rasterised again with `resvg -w 2400`.

The unchanged NIST session gate also passes in the complete suite. Three additional
serial runs, with no other work from this agent active, each measured nine deltas:
p90 **59.551, 80.857 and 57.344 ms**, below **150 ms**. Shared-machine load averages
at those starts were **31.27, 29.81 and 26.84**. These are isolated agent measurements,
not a quiet-machine claim; other users' load was not altered. No timing gate was
weakened. Logs: `target/g6-fix/latency-done-{1,2,3}.log`.

FTC-10 ink 23 against the previous documented 22.

endpoint alternatives, calculated from the fixed strokes actually crossing the
text box. It keeps the ordinary-ink and per-feature crossing floors and stays

rather than trim it through text. Python's fixed-component and actual-segment

## Stage H / G6 round 5: trim the axis, keep the label on its shelf (2026-10-12)

Review of 83875ad found that its FTC-10 repair only moved the defect: the text
hop (8525b11) and shelf shortening (83875ad) left hole 13's axis standing between
`⌀5.5 ↧ 35`'s shelf and its text. They also produced the same detached-label
pattern on string_post, CTC-05 and CTC-02, and a stub shelf on GRM-03. The cgb207
R5 leader still converged on hole 1's axis at a 1.2 degree tilt. Both leader
repairs are reverted in src/dims/leaders.rs.

- **FTC-10, ISO 128-24 trim.** After dimension placement, `centrelines::trim`
  shortens an axis end's overrun past its feature when a dimension label must
  stay across it. It stops 0.5 mm short of the label and keeps at least 1 mm past
  the feature. A label across the feature's own span is never trimmed around.
  `⌀5.5 ↧ 35` keeps its round-3 place, joined to its shelf 1 mm on. Hole 13's axis
  now runs y 245..314.33 (was 316), 1.33 mm past the hole. The same rule trims 11
  other axis ends by under 0.5 mm on CTC-05, FTC-07, FTC-08 and FTC-10's plan view.
  GD&T, notes and tables are placed after the trim and keep clear of the shortened axes.
- **Leaders beside centre lines.** `centrelines::runs_beside` flags a shaft that
  runs within 1 mm of a centre stroke at under 10 degrees for more than 0.5 mm.
  Such shafts are weighted just below one crossing; exact collinear travel stays
  hard. A radial leader leaving its own hole along that hole's centre-mark arm is
  exempt: counting it moved CTC-02 AP242's ⌀40 callouts into the GD&T tier and
  pushed datums E and J off the sheet. cgb207 R5 now leaves at about 11 degrees,
  7 mm clear at its elbow. string_post `⌀1.6 ↧ 4` leaves the A-A line, and FTC-10
  `8× ⌀2.5` leaves a vertical axis with a full shelf. Other-feature shaft-beside
  runs over the thirty parts: 30 to 27 (independent sampled count).

Sheets changed against the round-3 rebased render (`q0`): GRM-03, cgb207, CTC-05
(trims only), FTC-06, FTC-07 (trim only), FTC-08 (trims only), FTC-10 and
string_post. The other 22 SVGs are byte-identical. By-eye review of before/after
crops (scratchpad `g6r5/crops/`, plus FTC-10's side view): FTC-10's label reads
attached, with the axis stopping below it. R5, `⌀1.6` and `8× ⌀2.5` are clear of
the lines they ran along. FTC-06's `4× ⌀7.1` pattern callout leaves boss 6's line
and loses its 0.75 mm stub, but now runs down its own hole's arm (exempt).
GRM-03's ø6 takes the existing 0.75 mm shoulder escape. Its former shelf ran 1 mm
beside hole 1's axis, which is an existing hard blocker; this residual is
recorded in next-session.md. A shelf/label checker counting the reviewer's patterns (stroke through
text, stroke between shelf and text, shelf end within 1 mm of another leader,
shaft beside, stub shelf) finds 119 on the round-3 render and 112 now. Only the GRM-03
stub is new, and no stroke runs between a shelf and its text on any changed
callout. The six counterbored-hole sheets (CTC-02/04/05, FTC-06/07/10) were
reviewed: CTC-02 and CTC-04 are byte-identical to round 3, whose full recessed
axes were inspected then. CTC-05/FTC-07 differ only by trims of under 0.5 mm.

Regressions: `a_label_across_an_axis_end_trims_the_overrun` (fails on round 3:
axis end 316 inside the label; fails on 83875ad: text 2 mm off its shelf) and
`leaders_do_not_run_beside_centre_lines`, an angle-tolerant sampled check on
cgb207, cgb203 and string_post that fails on both earlier versions' R5. The new
unit tests are `labels_trim_only_an_axis_overrun` and
`leaders_beside_a_stroke_are_found_at_any_small_angle`. The FTC-10 shelf-gap and
six-sheet contact checks are kept.

## H5 fix round — reviewed ownership, reference ink and full gates (2026-10-12)

The committed attempt `312b23de` was not mergeable: claim transfer happened before
retention decisions, eccentric bands lost envelope sizes, functional ordinates
became references without support-plane evidence, and its verification was unfinished.
This round fixes those defects and retains the maintainer's named H5 behavior.
No commit, push, git-index mutation or MCP task-condition edit was made.

### Review disposition and evidence

- Claim transfer follows all retention checks. CTC-02 and FTC-10 each have zero
  repeated measurements again; the new cross-view-owner regression checks every
  drawn claim and fails against the committed attempt. Point ordinates are excluded
  by their actual `location.length.*` prefix; recognised geometry must independently
  prove the interval before it enters the graph. Recess positions, counted recipes
  and ordinary ladders retain their original contracts.
- Eccentric bands cannot use diameter as envelope evidence. The original drafting
  regression again requires all three X/Y/Z extents and four controlling band sizes.
  Its strengthened assertion fails against `312b23de`. No fixture-name exception
  exists in the planner.
- GRM-03 keeps 23.5 overall, with `(0.5)` plus controlling 2, 3 and 18, and no
  redundant 2.5 plate thickness. CTC-05 has one ø558.8 and one 527.05 A/F. The
  synthetic flush hub guards GRM-04's consolidated-height credit; no GRM-04 STEP is
  present. Spool132's three concentric bolt circles have six axis-location credits,
  independently retaining their diameter requirements. A box with all three overall
  sizes and a general tolerance is clear; omitting an overall loses stock-face credit
  and requires attention.
- Visual review found another 10047 closure: the 5.133348207668 mm front floor was
  still controlling beside `(5.1)` on the slot. Its slot location is 5.134052 mm,
  within 0.000704 mm of the same support planes. The feature reference now owns that
  floor claim too. If its location is explicitly omitted, the retained overall and
  functional slot length still prove the floor reference. The strengthened H5 test
  fails before this handover. The 24.2 overall and 19.1 functional length stay controlling.
- The unjustified `(8)` hole location, `(16)` ladders and `(12.5)` face/location
  statement are reverted. `dims_steps` again compares directly with the original
  Python facts; the value-based rewrite of every `16` is removed. GRM-05 and
  string_post legitimately follow the same general turned-chain rule: 14 + 5 + 13 =
  32 and 7.2 + 1.1 + 5.5 + 1 = 14.8. The actual Python differences have individual
  `rust-correct` verdicts with this geometry in `known_dimensions.json`, and their
  rendered sheets were reviewed. Equal rounded labels alone are never proof: 10060's
  two 19.3 statements belong to distinct 19.256 and 19.310 mm stations, so its
  repeated-label entry remains valid after restoring the physical ownership policy.
- On 10033 the R6.1 text, shoulder and slanted segment clear the 8.3 extensions and
  their one-millimetre pads. The old shaft missed the mathematical foot by roughly
  0.28 mm and read as contact. The stronger segment test fails on the committed
  attempt. A bounded final refinement applies only to a solitary already-clear
  callout in its view, with other ink fixed; it accepts no new crossing or area
  overrun. The original joint assignment objective is restored. Dense multi-callout
  views retain their established routing and require tables/details under sheet policy.
  FTC-07's 2× 9.3 uses the plan rim rather than a hidden hole-centreline witness.
- Reference-to-controlling edits change only the literal text bounds at the same
  centre; witnesses, arrows, lane and every unchanged label remain exact. Independent
  font-bounds assertions replace an impossible byte-equality assertion for removing
  parentheses. The native 10047 locality regression fails against the committed
  attempt. A bare pinned reference keeps its exact ink if another edit removes its
  controlling chain, with a warning `pinned_reference_chain_changed` and a tested
  executable unpin fix. Its own authored tolerance remains authoritative. Existing
  seeded pin geometry assertions are preserved.
- The trust test's existing alternate-chain clause is explicitly retained and
  reviewed: omission must leave independently drawn multi-annotation evidence whose
  envelope outcome is `Placed`; unchanged finding counts alone do not pass. The
  model fixture assertion now derives stock credit from `Analysis.stock_faces`,
  rather than fixture names. The S0 GRM-03 session benchmark retains its documented
  eight-operation workload despite the new overall carrier; all latency, delta and
  replay checks stay enabled. Exact FTC-07 recovery counts change from 16 to 14
  because the thickness view is corrected. Resolved band-reservation overruns are
  removed from `known_placement`; leader and structural residual fixtures are not
  loosened or expanded.

The failing-before evidence is in `target/h5-fix/before-regressions.log`,
`before-eccentric.log`, `before-reference-locality.log` and
`before-floor-ownership.log`. The first three logs exercise an archive of the committed
attempt without changing its checkout/index; the floor log exercises the immediately
preceding implementation before the additional visually discovered handover. After diagnosing a shared-target
archive build cache collision, only the package's release outputs were cleaned and
rebuilt from this worktree; the engine-version regression passed. Final gates use
that rebuilt source, with no subsequent source edits.

finishing quality command and independent ratchet were:

```sh
mkdir -p "$PWD/target/h5-fix/finish-quality"
C=/Users/paul/repos/quiddity/tests/corpus
sed "s#^#$C/#" tools/quality_parts.txt | tr '\n' '\0' | xargs -0 python3 -I tools/quality.py target/release/draftwright "$PWD/target/h5-fix/finish-quality"
python3 -I tools/check_quality_table.py target/h5-fix/status-before.md target/h5-fix/finish-quality/baseline.json
```

The nine grid invocations follow `tools/mcp-eval/README.md`, adding `--require-go`
and using `target/h5-fix/final-quality/baseline.json`; that baseline is verified
identical to the finishing baseline. All logs/artefacts live under `target/h5-fix`.
The scripted MCP evaluator is a test instrument, not a real-model success claim.

Remaining bounded scope is recorded in `known_h5.json`, the rule catalogue and
`docs/next-session.md`: arbitrary angle/pattern-pitch/per-hole ordinate and
counted/compound constraint graphs are not implemented. In particular cgb245's
8 + 364 = 372 point/pattern chain retains controlling functional coordinates until
that family has a reviewed ownership policy. Authored conflicting tolerances are
retained and reported by independent lint. GRM-04 has only the agreed synthetic
fallback because its STEP is absent. Dense-sheet allocation/tables, existing
recognition gaps and residual leader/section defects belong to their recorded
streams; this round does not claim those drawings are manufacturing-complete.
All reported H5 blockers and major fix-round issues are resolved; these are the
explicit remaining limits of the broader G12 port.

## H5 fix round 2 — slot-wall ladders, lint evidence, general leader rule (2026-10-12, Claude)

Review findings resolved:

- **10047 closed side chain** (major). Ladder rungs on a slot's walls close against the
  slot's stated width and centre line. 10047 now reads 4.5, 20.9, `(18.6)` and `(23.1)`.
  The same rule shows FTC-09's six slot-wall height rungs and FTC-10's 62.5 (= 12.5 + 50)
  as references. The extended lint also found 10060's 14.3 plate thickness (= 33.6 − 19.3
  shoulder). That plate is now covered by the shoulder chain: a riser at the plate face
  is support evidence across x or y.
- **Lint evidence for CTC-05** (major). `lint::redundancy::coverage` now reads outside
  diameters and A/F support planes (`external_span`), ladder rungs, slot widths and the
  centre line they imply, and slot centre-line locations. A regression test adds the
  former linear 558.8 and 527.0 to today's CTC-05 marks, and both are reported as
  `duplicate_measurement`. With every reference unparenthesised, 10047 reports
  `closed_dimension_chain`. All 30 quality sheets report neither code.
- **Decision (iii) as a general rule** (major). The solitary-callout, already-clear,
  non-PMI refinement is replaced. After the joint assignment, every callout in every
  view (semantic PMI included) moves to its best contact-free route that is no worse in
  any measure of the joint objective, with all other ink held fixed. Three variants
  that put contacts in the objective were measured and rejected, because each worsened
  quality cells on CTC-03/04/05 and FTC-06/07/08. `quality.py` gains
  `leader_on_extension`: 258 contacts before this round, 229 after. GRM-03, cgb207 and
  10033 are contact-free, and tests assert this.
- **Minor.** The session-edit helper's GRM-03 name check is replaced by a turned-part
  property.

The rule is general, but residual contacts remain. They are in dense views where every
contact-free route crosses another line, a feature or the area: CTC-02/04, FTC-08/09
and cgb245. They belong to the agreed table/detail allocation policy.

Verification (binary sha256 `cc9bf5e244aa2a56f6d9b6a951ecc4a7f1ee7831975bb57b2cf8ac62e8902dd1`):
fmt and strict release clippy pass. The full corpus suite with
`DRAFTWRIGHT_CORPUS_REQUIRED=1` passes 733 tests in 75 groups, with 0 failed and 0
ignored. Against the previous H5 table, 30 parts and 390 cells: no part worse. The
random-pin grid (seeds 1/2/3 × N = 1/3/8, `--require-go`) is GO in all nine runs. The
scripted MCP evaluation succeeds 7/7 with `tasks.json` unchanged. `tools/test_quality.py`
and `tools/test_ci_acceptance.py` pass. By eye, 10047, 10060, 10033, FTC-09, FTC-10,

## Stage H second pass: h-chains merged, h-extlines backed out (2026-10-13)

Merged into `main` (from 4017bca, Stage H integration plus the R5 docs commit) with
`--no-ff`: `cxg/h-chains` at c0849fc (closed-chain and duplicate removal, reference
dimensions, redundancy lint; its leader rule already split out to Stage I). Merge commit
d3c1fec. `cxg/h-extlines` at f706538 was merged on top, then **backed out**: after about
two hours its merged tree still failed six tests across four other streams' features
(below). The work in progress is kept, unpushed, on branch `stageh-pass2-extlines-wip`
(b0bb77c). Judgement calls and the h-extlines interaction list: `docs/next-session.md`,
"Stage H second pass".

## Stage I0 — preserve drawings while reducing planner work (2026-10-13; plan-time gate superseded by the I0 takeover entry)

Base: `0419fa6c1ebc81226cb577639276ea45eb9df3a1` (`origin/main` at stream start).
No commits or pushes were made. The sheet/scale search, candidate order, score,
deduplication, intersection predicates and drafting output are preserved.

`src/dims/footprints.rs` caches polygon bounds and exact shaft crossing counts
within the resolved view, with a conservative sorted-bounds broad phase. Unbounded
or non-finite evidence falls back to the full scan. `src/dims/leaders.rs` merges
geometric lower bounds with exact refinements until the original retained prefix
is known. It avoids the unused baseline-aware inventory for semantic PMI and
caches immutable conflict geometry. It computes each nearest-site distance once.
No dependencies, part/ID exceptions, drafting thresholds or information removals.

Neither samply nor cargo-flamegraph was available; macOS `sample` could not examine
the process. Temporary timing/call-count instrumentation supplied the profile and
was removed before building the final binary. STC-09 e4 makes 272 leader placement
calls during the unchanged sheet search. The baseline made 8,193,357 footprint
queries / 335,927,637 feature tests; lazy refinement makes at most 293,773 feature
measurement calls and 1,839,027 fixed-ink measurements, instead of approximately
8.2 million of each. Diagnostic instrumented leader time was 31.881 → 5.454 s;
baseline crossing time was 19.514 s, and final feature measurement time was 0.074 s.
These profile times were collected under load and are **not latency acceptance**.

`tools/check_plan_identity.py` compares raw CLI plan JSON and every SVG sheet over
the 64 survey parts, checking all 30 quality parts are included. It neither
normalizes JSON nor removes SVG content. `tools/check_plan_time.py` sums serial
CLI wall time and divides by the median of six same-run calibration plans (three
before / three after). Calibration is `threaded_connector_109.step` planning,
without HLR. CI's acceptance matrix runs the new budget job; its limit is the
recorded working-tree ratio plus 15%. `tools/test_ci_acceptance.py` adds a wiring
test which failed on base (missing `plan-time` job) before passing on this tree.
The identity oracle necessarily passes base versus itself; damaged JSON and SVG
references were rejected as negative controls. See `docs/next-session.md` for the
overturnable calibration and scope judgements.

Final output verification: `target/i0/resumed-identity.log` passes all 64 parts /
64 SVG sheets against the base capture, including all 30 quality parts. Both
captures contain 1,383 dimensions and 329 balloons. The final 30 quality sheets
and STC-09 e4, CTC-02 e2, STC-10 e2 were rasterized with resvg at width 2400;
all SVG and PNG bytes match (`target/i0/final-visual/comparison.json`). The 30
quality before/after contact sheets and the three named full sheets were examined:
leader lengths/routes, hidden/section/cutting-plane crossings, text, radius tables
and section hatch are unchanged. No output was removed to improve a metric.

Final MCP validation passes 7/7 unchanged tasks with 16 calls and 6,006 estimated
tokens (`target/i0/resumed-mcp/summary.json`); client tests pass 8/8. Python quality
tests pass 34/34 and acceptance tests 18/18. Both added acceptance tests fail on a
complete archived base checkout: the CI job and budget tool are absent. The report
test also rejects missing parts and aggregates that disagree with samples.
Schema generation adds no diff and
`tasks.json` is unchanged. Release fmt and all-target clippy with `-D warnings`
pass (`target/i0/verified-{fmt,clippy}.log`).

The pin grid already failed on base for native FTC-07 at seed 2 / N=3 and N=8:
ink 1 → 4, off-sheet 0, with four `annotation_outside_area` findings. The final
binary reproduces both base pinned Plans byte for byte, each with 40 dimensions
(`target/i0/final-pin-reproduction.log`). Changing these layouts would violate
this stream's preservation requirement. The pin bar and selection remain intact;
this existing failure is reported, not hidden or weakened.

All 270 saved pinned Specs from the completed grid were replanned by the final
binary using their saved Analyses; every Plan matches the grid's serialized Plan
(`target/i0/final-pin-plan-identity.log`). This extra preservation check avoids
repeating HLR and does not claim a new render/grid run. The complete earlier grid
passes seven cells / fails two (above). A repeated `--job all --require-go` run
hit the quality step's 1,500 s timeout under load near 60; its automatic locality
fallback began a second quality render, so that redundant attempt was interrupted.
The already completed final-binary quality run passes all 300 cells exactly,
including ink 52, feature-line crossings 73 and off-sheet 0
(`target/i0/final-quality-validation.json`).

The captured final-binary serial timing report sums 352.271 s, with median
calibration 5.710 s, ratio 61.689 and maximum load 74.57. These are diagnostic
figures: low-load latency acceptance is **unmeasured** until a run stays below 8.
Rechecking that actual report passes the recorded ratio's 70.943 limit and fails
when the budget is lowered 30% (limit 49.660), exits 0 and 1 respectively
(`target/i0/{head,lowered}-budget-control.log`). CI still executes every plan;
`--results` only rechecks a complete, internally consistent measured report.

All four repeated-spans CI commands pass against the same final release binary:
shards 1/4 and 4/4 in `target/i0/final-survey-{1,4}`, and resumed shards 2/4 and
3/4 in `target/i0/resumed-survey-{2,3}`. Each has all 16 expected parts and zero
repeated spans (64/64 total). Wall times under load were 451.161, 5,468.772,
4,039.173 and 544.686 s respectively; these are functional receipts, not accepted
latency measurements. The interrupted attempts were not counted as passes.

## I0 fix round — Codex progress log (2026-10-13; plan-time calibration and per-part statements superseded by the I0 takeover entry below)

The earlier work did not meet the measured complete-CLI target (review: 0.717 of BASE). This round keeps BASE at 0419fa6c and adds reuse across H6's search: model-derived wants, demand and lanes; model-space interior evidence; exact duplicate requested candidates; and finished ink for arrangement requests resolving to identical geometry. Each returned candidate retains its own sheet/view basis and arrangement. Preparation is scoped to an immutable Analysis borrow and is discarded on return/unwind; pins bypass finished-ink reuse.

A new profile of STC-10 e2 found 212 internal passes. After preparation reuse there are 196, and substantial time remains in radius-table/general-note placement. `layout::fit_box` materialised and sorted the whole X×Y product on every attempt. It now merges one Y stream per X through a heap, retaining the old `(distance, X, Y)` order and explicitly sorting rounded sum ties by Y. Non-finite inputs retain the original full sort. The Python reference already uses the corresponding heap merge (`layout.py`, issue #1145); Rust keeps an additional sum-rounding tie guard for byte preservation.

Measured saved-Analysis planning instructions against the earlier worktree binary: FTC-10 e2 180.700 → 149.072 billion with preparation reuse; STC-10 e2 328.538 → 287.468 billion with preparation reuse, then 169.299 billion with identical resolved-candidate reuse. These are diagnostics, **not complete-CLI survey acceptance**. Complete survey, output identity, quality and full-suite receipts are pending under `target/i0-fix/`.

`tools/check_plan_instructions.py` counts unreaped children using Darwin's retired-instruction counter, validates raw output equality, records every part, and can enforce both the 55% aggregate and strict per-part bar. The sandbox prevents `/usr/bin/time -l` from completing its clock sysctl, although child-process rusage remains available. Repeated complete-CLI 10038 controls show roughly 3–5% instruction savings from preparation reuse.

The plan-time calibration is now a fingerprinted frozen legacy Analysis/2 without feature leaders or sheet search, with A3 / 1:1 / no hole table. Replaying 32,768 fixed plan requests in one `serve` process amortises startup and short scheduling gaps, and division by 32,768 retains per-plan units; explicit scale prevents recovery memoisation. It is independent of the survey. A fresh final budget and new-run lowered-budget control remain pending; load above 30 does not establish quiet-machine/CI latency acceptance.

`--require-go` now requires the complete nine-cell grid and rejects partial/unrelated jobs. The Python acceptance tests pass 21/21 (with the corpus required); the eight MCP client tests also pass. Release all-target Clippy with warnings denied and formatting pass. The optimized placement oracle tests are building; final release tests and other required checks remain pending. No commit or push has been made.

Fix-round verification checkpoint: the final Rust binary is unchanged since the release build. Formatting, all-target release Clippy, the two old-sort placement oracle tests, the two preparation-scope tests, Python quality 34/34, Python acceptance 21/21, MCP client 8/8 and scripted MCP 7/7 pass. Schema generation and tasks.json have no diff. The fresh 30-part quality run has no worse cell (300/300), and seed 1 pin cells N=1/3/8 pass. Shard 1/4 passes all 16 parts. Plan/SVG/raster identity is complete for 62/64 so far; the connector render is still running. One clean full release test invocation, remaining shards/grid, instruction survey and fresh lowered-budget run are still active. A fresh budget run recorded normalised_total 10,218,707.369 with maximum load 43.98; this is diagnostic, not quiet-machine acceptance. Receipts remain under target/i0-fix/.

Preservation completed: final release CLI plans and every rendered SVG match 0419fa6c byte-for-byte for all 64 survey parts, including all 30 quality parts. Every sheet was rasterised with `resvg -w 2400`; all BASE/working-tree PNG bytes match. Paired white-paper contact pages 1–8 were inspected, with full-size STC-09 e4, STC-10 e2, CTC-02 e2 and FTC-10 e2 checks. The connector and thumbwheel complete page 8. No information was removed. Receipts: target/i0-fix/identity.log and target/i0-fix/visual/comparison.json. Seed 2 / N=1 also passes the fresh grid; N=3 is active.

Fresh lowered-budget control **did not reject**: a second actual 64-part CLI run took 3,097.398 s versus 2,201.332 s in the recorded run, but calibration median rose from 0.000215422 to 0.001151197 s/plan. Normalised total fell to 2,690,590.180, below the lowered limit 8,226,059.432; exit 0. Maximum load rose to 61.92, and calibration samples span about 0.000174–0.002431 s/plan. This is an unmet budget-control/quiet-run acceptance item, not a green proof; the median algorithm and 15% threshold are unchanged. Both distinct raw reports are retained as target/i0-fix/budget-head.json and budget-control-fresh.json.

The fresh native FTC-07 seed 2 / N=3 spec was planned directly by both 0419fa6c and the working-tree binary: raw CLI Plan JSON bytes match, and the result equals the fresh grid Plan. Receipts: target/i0-fix/ftc07-pin-2-3-{base,head}.json and ftc07-pin-2-3-identity.json. The grid cell itself is still running other parts.

The fresh seed 2 / N=3 grid completes with exactly one NO-GO: native FTC-07 ink 1 → 4, off-sheet 0 → 0; other 29 parts pass. Fresh BASE/current native plans for that exact spec are byte-identical (see preceding receipt). This preserves the pre-existing failure rather than weaken GO or change a Plan. Seed 2 / N=8 is now running.

First complete paired survey: total instruction ratio 0.542305441 (55% aggregate target met); strict per-part check fails on cgb203 +0.0659%, native CTC-02 +11.1811%, threaded_connector_109 +0.0295%. STC-09 e4 is 757.415 → 153.449 billion (0.2026), STC-10 e2 460.335 → 127.705 billion (0.2774), FTC-10 e2 1,305.177 → 1,110.205 billion (0.8506). The external BASE path disappeared after 57 rows; the seven remaining rows use the private clean-BASE copy described in next-session.md. The interrupted report/log and resumed report/log are retained separately. This is not a green per-part acceptance. Uniform repeats are being started against the protected BASE copy.

Fresh shard 2/4 completes PASS: all 16 parts, zero repeated spans (6,658.799 s under load). Shard 3/4 is active. The seed 2 / N=8 grid likewise has exactly one NO-GO, native FTC-07 ink 1 → 4 with off-sheet 0 → 0; fresh BASE/current native raw Plan JSON bytes match and match the grid Plan (target/i0-fix/ftc07-pin-2-8-{base,head}.json and ftc07-pin-2-8-identity.json). Other 29 parts pass this cell. The two original pin failures are reproduced, with no GO threshold change.

## I0 takeover — final state (Claude, 2026-10-14)

Branch `cxi/i0-speed`, rebased onto origin/main d1193921. Receipts are in
`target/i0-claude/` of the stream worktree.

- Plan-time gate: `tools/check_plan_time.py` now sums retired instructions from
  `draftwright plan` over the 64 survey parts. It fails when the total is more than
  15% above the figure in `tools/plan-time-budget.json` for this platform. Where
  there is no counter or no figure (CI's Linux runners), it reports wall time and
  passes. Recorded at HEAD: darwin-arm64 3,177,525,490,268. Fresh real runs:
  HEAD 3,177,472,420,512 (1.000, PASS); HEAD against the budget lowered by 30%
  3,177,886,963,129 (1.429 of the lowered figure, FAIL, exit 1); the pre-stream
  0419fa6c binary (a genuinely slower build) 6,066,017,147,918 (1.909, FAIL,
  exit 1). Totals agree within 0.02% across runs at load 8 to 60.
- Instructions vs d1193921, a single paired run: total ratio 0.524; no part is
  worse, even under the strict limit (worst: cgb203 0.9996). `--check` exits 0.
- Byte identity vs d1193921: plan JSON and every SVG match for all 64 survey parts,
  including the 30 quality parts.
- Checks: cargo fmt and clippy (`-D warnings`) pass; cargo test --release has 81
  suites ok; test_quality 34/34; test_ci_acceptance 21/21. `ci_acceptance.py --job
  all --require-go` passes: quality has no worse cell, all 9 pin cells pass
  (FTC-07 2-3 and 2-8 pass after the rebase), and the survey, MCP (client tests and
  scripted evaluation) and plan-time jobs pass. All four repeated-spans shards pass
  on their own. Schema generation leaves no diff, and tasks.json is unchanged.


## I1: multi-sheet model and allocation (2026-10-13)

Implemented R5's I1 model, floor-aware cumulative allocation, later-sheet packing,
numbering, zones, cross-reference data, rescue-table outcomes and sheet assignment,
per-sheet audits, item sheet derivation and edit validation. Rendering still draws
sheet 1; I2 owns later-sheet furniture/output, and I3 owns MCP/session extensions.
The H6/bounded-ladder reconciliation and other overturnable judgements are in
`docs/next-session.md`.

Changed files: `src/sheets.rs` (new); `src/model.rs`, `src/layout.rs`,
`src/hole_table.rs`, `src/items.rs`, `src/tables.rs`, `src/lib.rs`,
`src/dims/{placement,mod,gdt,blends}.rs`; one default field in the existing
`src/centrelines.rs` test and in `tests/{dimension_rules,hole_tables,page_escalation,
pmi_labels,render_caches}.rs`; `tests/sheets.rs`, `tests/common/single_sheet.rs` and
`tests/fixtures/known_single_sheet{,_specs}.json` (new); `.github/workflows/ci.yml`;
`docs/model.md`, this status entry and `docs/next-session.md`.

The first change recorded base `0419fa6` outputs before allocation work. The final
frozen manifest has 1,876 cases: 1,677 successful plan/SVG/PDF/macOS-PNG documents,
198 refusals and one explicit pre-existing native-replay panic (see the handoff).
Its SHA-256 is `88b83ded35028e9e724dda8c04ac1e415e9ba15a9f4a070c8001c5dd8ecb4db7`.
The existing base suite passed all 822 tests while capturing its actual Specs.
`known_single_sheet_specs.json` contains 1,875 captured Specs over 63 source digests.
The guard compares portable plan/SVG/PDF bytes on every platform and PNG only on
recorded platforms; the opt-in Linux workflow uploads the extended JSON, retaining
the macOS PNG hashes. Linux recording has not been run locally.

Added 23 R5 integration tests, two focused packing unit tests, and the byte guard.
Eight behavior tests compiled and failed on base (`/tmp/i1-red.log`). The final
integration file was also checked against an API built from archived `origin/main`
source: it fails compilation because the new sheet fields/checks/outcomes do not
exist (`/tmp/i1-red-api-final.log`). The three search Specs and the authored-label
Specs were separately refused by the frozen base CLI. These are explicit API/red
proofs, not a claim that every typed test executed on base. The byte invariant
intentionally passes on base. No existing assertion or quality baseline was weakened.

Drawing review: all 30 default release-binary SVGs and Plans are byte-identical to
base. The complete measured quality reports are equal, including the non-gated
leader/extension metric. All 30 sheets were rasterised with `resvg -w 2400` and
inspected before and after; contacts are under `target/i1-before` and `target/i1-after`.
